  The .txt file in the project is a saved transcript of your earlier session. It
  holds the full brief and the Figma/Pixso design work, so I have the "facts
  above" your paste refers to. Here is what I understand, so you can correct it.

  What you said:
  - Build the Kvarta roastery landing page (fictional Minsk client, Russian
    copy, lang="ru") in Next.js with Material UI.
  - Make small Git commits as I go, each with a one-sentence message.
  - Deploy it.
  - Requirements from the brief:
    - Mobile Lighthouse 90+ in all four categories.
    - Optimized images and self-hosted fonts.
    - Animations that respect prefers-reduced-motion.
    - Accessibility, SEO and Open Graph tags.

  What I took from the transcript:
  - The brief is a junior+ test task due Mon 19 Oct 2026, 18:00 Moscow time.
  - The required sections are story, range with prices, subscription, delivery
    and pickup, contacts, and a lead form.
  - POST /api/lead must meet these rules:
    - Phone must be a Belarusian number.
    - The same phone within 10 minutes creates no second lead, including on a
      double click.
    - Attribution (UTM, yclid, fbclid, referrer) is captured.
    - A send failure shows an error with a next step.
  - The visual direction is a printed-label and Thursday-roast concept. It uses
    cream and espresso colours, a roast-red quarter-circle mark, and Literata,
    Onest and JetBrains Mono fonts. The layouts are mobile 390, tablet 768 and
    desktop 1440, in Figma and Pixso.

  My assumptions, which I'll put in the spec and README:
  - Storage: Vercel's filesystem is ephemeral, so a file or local SQLite would
    lose leads. I'd use a hosted store with an atomic 10-minute dedupe key.
    Upstash Redis fits well, and Turso is the alternative.
  - Hosting: Vercel.
  - Telegram: duplicate each lead to a Telegram bot, with the token in an
    environment variable. Telegram failing must never fail the lead.
  - Material UI: I'd use it with a custom theme built from the design tokens, so
    the page doesn't look like default MUI.
  - Unconfirmed brief details: the Wednesday 20:00 order cutoff, delivery cost
    below 50 BYN, landline policy and subscription pricing. These stay as
    labelled assumptions.

  One question that changes what I do next: which accounts can I deploy with?
  - Vercel: is the vercel CLI logged in, or should I give you the steps to run
    yourself? It isn't installed here.
  - Git remote: do you want a public GitHub repo? The brief asks for a repo
    link, and gh isn't installed either.
  - Store: do you already have an Upstash or Turso account?

  Once you answer, I'll move on to approaches and the sectioned design.

● Understood: the requirements are final and match the brief I read from the
  transcript. You didn't answer the accounts question, so it's still the one
  thing blocking deployment. I'll continue with the design in the meantime.

  Approaches

  A. Next.js (App Router) + MUI + Upstash Redis on Vercel (recommended)
  - POST /api/lead is a route handler. Dedupe is a single atomic SET 
    phone:<E164> NX EX 600. Leads are stored in a Redis list or hash.
  - Why: it's the shortest path to atomic dedupe and a working form on the live
    link. Upstash has a free tier and a Vercel integration. Next.js is also the
    stack the task owner prefers.
  - Cost: Redis isn't a relational store, so a lead is a JSON blob. That's fine
    for a callback list.

  B. Same stack, Turso (SQLite) instead of Redis
  - Dedupe uses a unique constraint plus a time window. Leads are queryable with
    SQL.
  - Why not: the dedupe logic is more code and more room for race-condition
    bugs. It's more than this task needs.

  C. Cloudflare Pages + D1
  - It's free and fast.
  - Why not: the Next.js and MUI setup on Cloudflare's edge runtime is riskier,
    and the deadline is tight.

  Recommendation: A. Redis gives atomic dedupe in one call, which is the hard
  requirement (F2).

  Design decisions I'll carry into the spec

  - MUI with a custom theme. The design tokens (cream, espresso, roast-red,
    yolk-yellow, Literata, Onest, JetBrains Mono) go into createTheme. Fonts are
    self-hosted via next/font. The MUI SSR registry is the Next.js App Router
    setup, which avoids a flash of unstyled content and layout shift.
  - Lighthouse risk. MUI plus Emotion adds JavaScript weight. I'll keep most
    sections as server components and make only the form, the countdown and the
    sticky CTA client components. I'll measure early rather than at the end.
  - Section order. Hero, "our week" (Thursday roast to Friday delivery), range,
    subscription, delivery and pickup, FAQ, lead form, footer and contacts. A
    sticky CTA appears on mobile.
  - Lead API order of operations. Validate, check the honeypot and fill time,
    rate-limit by IP, then dedupe, save, and finally notify Telegram. A Telegram
    failure never fails the lead.
  - Duplicate response. A duplicate returns the original success response, and
    the client also sends an idempotency key.

  One question

  Can you deploy to Vercel and push to GitHub, and if so, how?

  1. You log in and I run the commands. Run ! npx vercel login and ! npx gh auth
     login in this prompt, or install gh first.
  2. I prepare everything, and you run the final deploy and push yourself.
  3. You want a different host.

  For Redis, I'll provision Upstash through Vercel's integration unless you
  already have an account.

  12', '291234567' gives '+375 29 123-45-67', and '' or junk gives ''.

  wrapped in try/catch.

    - Full capture of all params plus landing and referrer.
    - Empty query string yields only landing and referrer.
    - First-touch: a second call with different params returns the first values.
    - A value over 300 characters is truncated.
    - Corrupt stored JSON recovers.
    - Storage that throws does not throw and returns fresh values.
    - `readAttribution` returns {} when storage is empty, corrupt or throwing.

› Message from general-purpose
  ### Spec Compliance
  - ✅ Spec compliant. `normalizeBelarusPhone` and `formatPhoneMask` are
  exported from `lib/phone.ts` with the required signatures. Letters, emoji and
  punctuation-only input all return null, and nothing throws.
  - ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: I did not run `npm test -- phone`, so the 26/26
  pass and the RED run rest on the report. The code and test values below were
  checked by reading only.

  Checks, each traced by hand:
  1. Length rules in `lib/phone.ts:33-42`.
     - Accepted: '80291234567' (11 digits, starts 80), '375291234567' (12,
  starts 375), '291234567' (9) and '+375 29 123-45-67' all give `+375291234567`.
     - Rejected, all null:
       - '801234567': 9 digits with code 80.
       - a 12-digit number not starting 375.
       - 10 and 13 digits.
       - codes 17, 99 and 00.
       - '8 029 123 45 67' is accepted (digits 80291234567).
     - No wrong accepts or rejects found.
  2. Subscriber digits that look like a prefix.
     - '293751234' normalizes to `+375293751234`. The mask gives `+375 29
  375-12-34`. `stripPrefix` only looks at the start of the string, so it does
  not mis-strip.
     - '337508888' gives `+375337508888` and `+375 33 750-88-88`. Code 33 starts
  '33', not '375'.
     - Letters and emoji never reach the digit logic. `[^0-9]` is ASCII-only, so
  Arabic-Indic digits and the Cyrillic letter 'yo' are stripped and give null. This matches the test
  at `tests/phone.test.ts:95`.
  3. `formatPhoneMask`.
     - Partial input, truncation to 9 digits and idempotence on its own output
  all hold. For example '+375 2' becomes digits 3752, then '2', then '+375 2'.
     - **Typing is not coherent.** The mask is applied on every keystroke to the
  whole field value. A lone '8' or '80' strips to '' and clears the field, so
  typing "80291..." digit by digit yields '+375 0', '+375 02' and so on.
     - Typing the prefix by hand has the same problem. '+3' gives '+375 3', then
  '+375 37', then '+375 375'. Paste and autofill work.
     - The implementer flagged the lone '8' case. Beyond '8', manually typed
  '+375' is the more common input and is also wrong.
  4. Tests.
     - Expected values are literal and meaningful, not tautologies.
     - The RED evidence is credible: an unresolved `@/lib/phone` import is the
  expected first failure.

  ### Strengths
  - The normalizer is strict and simple. The length and prefix rules are
  explicit and match the brief exactly.
  - No path can throw. `digitsOf` also guards non-string input.
  - Test coverage goes beyond the brief: all four codes, 10 subscriber digits,
  Arabic-Indic digits, partial masks, truncation and prefix handling.
  - The commit is a single file pair with a one-sentence message.

  ### Issues
  #### Critical (Must Fix)
  - None.

  #### Important (Should Fix)
  - `lib/phone.ts:24-30` and `:45-47`: `formatPhoneMask` cannot be used on
  keystrokes for a number typed from the start with an '8' or '+375' prefix
  (traces in check 3).
    - A fix would strip a prefix only when the digit string is long enough to be
  unambiguous (at least 10 digits for '80', at least 12 for '375').
  Alternatively, the Task 3 form could apply the mask only to pasted or complete
  input.
    - Settle this before Task 3, which will use the mask on every keystroke.

  #### Minor (Nice to Have)
  - `tests/phone.test.ts`: there are no negative tests for 9 digits starting 80,
  for a 12-digit number not starting 375, or for 11 digits not starting 80.
  - `tests/phone.test.ts`: there is no test for the trap inputs '293751234' and
  '337508888'.
  - `tests/phone.test.ts`: there is no idempotence test (`mask(mask(x)) ===
  mask(x)`).
  - `tests/phone.test.ts`: there is no test pinning the lone '8' and '+375'
  typing behaviour, so the Important issue above is not locked in either way.
  - `lib/phone.ts:20-22`: the `typeof input === 'string'` guard is untested. It
  is harmless.

  ### Assessment
  **Task quality:** Approved
  **Reasoning:** The normalizer meets every stated requirement and the spec
  focus (junk gives null, never throws). The mask is correct for paste, partial
  and complete input but breaks when a number is typed digit by digit from a
  leading 8 or +375. Flag that for Task 3 and adjust the mask before the form
  uses it live.

✻ Worked for 2m 14s · done 20:23

› Message from general-purpose
  ### Spec Compliance
  - ✅ Spec compliant. All five functions are exported and pure, and they take
  `now: Date`. The constants are named exports. The offset is fixed at UTC+3
  with no library and no Intl. The brief's boundary cases are covered: Thu 08:59
  and 09:00, Wed 19:59 and 20:00, and the isOpenNow cases.
  - ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: I did not run `npm test -- schedule`. The report
  claims 27 passing. All traces below were done by reading the code.

  Hand traces (lib/schedule.ts):
  1. nextRoast. `nextWeekly` uses `minskMidnight` and `getUTCDay` on the
  +3h-shifted Date. There are no local getters anywhere, so host time zone
  cannot matter.
     - Thu 08:59:59 Minsk: dow=4, daysAhead=0, target = today 09:00 > now, so it
  returns today 09:00.
     - Thu 09:00:00: target <= now, so +7, giving Thu 22 Oct 09:00. 09:00:01
  gives the same result.
     - Wed 22:30 UTC: shifted is Thu 01:30, so dow=4, daysAhead=0, and it
  returns Thu 09:00 Minsk (06:00Z). Correct.
     - Sun 23:59 Minsk: dow=0, daysAhead=(4-0+7)%7=4, so it returns Thursday of
  the new week. Correct.
     - Thu 31 Dec: this is pure millisecond arithmetic (`DAY_MS` multiples). The
  midnight mod handles negative and positive values, and there is no DST, so
  month and year ends are safe.
  2. orderCutoff, from reading the code (it returns the Wednesday two days
  before `nextDelivery(now)`, at 20:00):
     - It matches the brief, but the value is coherent only while `now <
  cutoff`, meaning from Fri 12:00 to Wed 20:00. Between Wed 20:00 and Fri 12:00
  it returns a PAST instant. At exactly Wed 20:00 it equals `now`.
     - Trace: Thu 10:00 gives nextRoast = Thu 22 Oct, but nextDelivery = Fri 16
  Oct, so the cutoff = Wed 14 Oct (past). A page that shows "orders until Wednesday, 20:00" next to nextRoast would show a stale date from Thu 09:00 to Fri 12:00.
  It would pair a roast a week out with a cutoff that has already passed. Wed
  20:00 to Thu 09:00 is also in the past, but there it at least pairs with the
  imminent roast.
  3. isOpenNow and hoursLabel. The window is `t >= open*60 && t < close*60`, so
  opening is inclusive and closing is exclusive.
     - Mon 20:00: closed.
     - Sun 18:00: closed, and the label is hoursFor(1).open = "we open at
  08:00". Correct.
     - Fri 20:00: closed, and the label is hoursFor(6).open = "we open at
  10:00". Correct.
     - Sat 09:00: t<open, so "we open at 10:00". Correct.
     - Sat 19:00: hoursFor(0).open, so "10:00". Correct.
     - Sun 23:00: Monday, "08:00". Correct.
     - Mon 00:30 Minsk, which is Sun 21:30 UTC: closed, "08:00". Correct.
  4. Tests. Expected values come from a separate `minsk()` helper built on
  `Date.UTC(h-3)`, not from the implementation's formula, plus a few raw UTC
  instants. They are not tautologies. The Review Focus boundaries are covered,
  with the gaps listed under Minor.

  ### Strengths
  - The offset math is small and correct. It uses only getUTC* on the shifted
  date, and the strictly-after semantics are simple and consistent.
  - `minsk()` in the tests is an independent oracle.
  - The tests include UTC-vs-Minsk date-mismatch cases and a no-mutation check.
  The business constants are named exports.

  ### Issues
  #### Critical (Must Fix)
  - None.

  #### Important (Should Fix)
  - lib/schedule.ts:71-76. `orderCutoff` returns a past instant from Wed 20:00
  to Fri 12:00. From Thu 09:00 to Fri 12:00, `nextRoast` (next week) and
  `orderCutoff` (this past Wednesday) refer to different weeks. A consumer that
  renders "orders until Wednesday, 20:00" from this value gets a misleading date, and
  nothing in the API or its comments warns about it.
    - Smallest fix that keeps the brief's semantics: add a doc comment saying
  the value is only a deadline while `now < orderCutoff(now)`. Also export
  `isOrderOpen(now) { return now.getTime() < orderCutoff(now).getTime(); }`, so
  the UI chooses between "orders until Wednesday, 20:00" and a "this week's roast is
  closed" state. Do not change `orderCutoff` to roll forward: the brief requires
  Wed 20:00 sharp to be "not before the cutoff" and tests assert it.

  #### Minor (Nice to Have)
  - tests/schedule.test.ts:
    - There is no Sun 23:59 nextRoast week-crossing test.
    - There is no month- or year-end test (for example Thu 31 Dec 2026 or Wed 30
  Dec).
    - There is no :59 seconds case, so Thu 08:59:59 and 09:00:01 are untested;
  only 08:59 and 09:00:00 are.
    - There is no test for the orderCutoff window from Wed 20:01 to Fri 11:59.
  It would pin the documented past-instant behavior.
    - There is no explicit hoursLabel test at Fri 20:00 sharp or Sun 18:00
  sharp. isOpenNow does cover Sun 18:00.
    - These all pass by inspection, but a regression there would go unnoticed.
  - lib/schedule.ts: the header comment says "cited in the README", but no
  README is part of this diff.

  ### Assessment
  **Task quality:** Needs fixes
  **Reasoning:** The core date arithmetic is correct, and all boundary traces
  hold with no local-time getters. The one real defect is that `orderCutoff`
  returns a stale past date in a roughly 40-hour window (Wed 20:00 to Fri 12:00)
  that a consumer pairing it with `nextRoast` will display wrongly. Add a doc
  comment and an `isOrderOpen` helper before the UI tasks consume it.

  ### Spec Compliance
  - ✅ Spec compliant. All the controller's clarifications are met: key
  `kvarta_attr`, the 8 whitelisted params plus `landing` and `referrer`,
  first-touch, 300-char cap, storage failures never throw, corrupt JSON treated
  as empty, and a `'use client'` component that renders null. The component has
  both a named and a default export.
  - ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: I did not re-run the suite, so the report's 7/7
  pass, lint and build results are unconfirmed. I also did not check the lead
  form or layout wiring (later tasks), or whether the layout places
  `<AttributionCapture />` correctly under Cache Components. The component uses
  no `useSearchParams`, so it should need no Suspense boundary.

  ### Strengths
  - `lib/attribution.ts:84-85`: first-touch is a single early return, which is
  simple and matches the brief literally.
  - `readAttribution` (`:62-76`) guards every failure path with one try/catch.
  It rejects `null`, arrays and primitives (`"null"`, `"[]"`, `"5"` all give
  `{}`) and keeps only string values.
  - `captureAttribution` wraps `setItem` separately (`:96-100`), so a throwing
  `getItem` and `setItem` still returns the fresh values.
  - The first test (`tests/attribution.test.ts:138-152`) uses `toEqual` on the
  exact object, including `other=x`. That proves the whitelist and gives a real
  value assertion.

  ### Issues
  #### Critical (Must Fix)
  - None.

  #### Important (Should Fix)
  - None.

  #### Minor (Nice to Have)
  - **Risk 1, first-touch semantics.** An organic first visit stores `{landing,
  referrer}` and counts as "values present" (`lib/attribution.ts:85`). A later
  page view carrying `utm_*` or `yclid` in the same tab session is therefore
  discarded. This is a literal reading of "keep first-touch values" and of the
  brief ("if storage already holds values, return unchanged"), so it is
  defensible. It is not documented anywhere, though. A one-line comment at `:84`
  would stop a later maintainer from "fixing" it. Upgrading an organic-only
  record when campaign keys arrive would be a product decision, not something
  this task needs.
  - **Risk 3, empty and whitespace values.** `?utm_source=` is correctly not
  stored (`:91`, falsy check). `?utm_source=%20` is stored as a space, and `+`
  decodes to a space via `URLSearchParams`. A `.trim()` would fix it.
  - **Risk 3, `slice` and unicode.** `slice(0, 300)` counts UTF-16 units and can
  cut a surrogate pair in half. This is harmless for the server's length cap,
  and the result may be an invalid string.
  - **Risk 3, repeated params.** `?utm_source=a&utm_source=b` stores `a`
  (`params.get` returns the first). That is sane, but no test covers it.
  - **Risk 4, `readAttribution` on stored data.** It passes through any
  string-valued key found in storage, not just the whitelist plus `landing` and
  `referrer`. Storage is first-party, so the risk is low. The later server
  schema should not rely on the client having filtered keys.
  - **Risk 6, test gaps.** The tests do not cover:
    - `?utm_source=a&evil=b` on its own (the `other=x` case only partly covers
  it);
    - the empty-value case;
    - percent-encoded values;
    - repeated params;
    - stored `"null"`, `"[]"` or `"5"` (only `{not json` and `nope` are tested);
    - a store where only `setItem` throws;
    - the case where an organic-only first touch is kept after a later campaign
  visit.

    The first-touch test (`:159-165`) does not assert the concrete first value
  (`utm_source: 'ya'`). It is not a tautology, because the second call's inputs
  differ, but a literal assertion would be stronger.
  - **Risk 2, StrictMode.** The second effect run reads the stored object and
  returns early, so it is idempotent and traced as a no-op. If `setItem` throws,
  both runs recompute the same values, which is also harmless. No test
  exercises the double call on the component, and the first-touch test covers
  the equivalent logic.
  - **Risk 5, SSR.** `window`, `document` and `sessionStorage` are touched only
  inside `useEffect` (`components/AttributionCapture.tsx:24-36`). The try/catch
  also covers the case where reading `window.sessionStorage` throws. No SSR or
  prerender hazard.

  ### Assessment
  **Task quality:** Approved
  **Reasoning:** The implementation matches the brief and all six named risks
  trace correctly: the whitelist, empty-value handling, throwing storage and
  non-object JSON all behave as intended. The remaining items are an
  undocumented first-touch decision and test-coverage gaps.

  ### Finding Verdicts

  start with an '8' or '+375' prefix (lone '8'/'80' cleared the field; '+3'
  became '+375 3')** — ADDRESSED. `lib/phone.ts` now returns `+` for a bare plus
  (`:31`). It keeps `+`, `+3`, `+37` and `+375` unchanged (`:34`), keeps `8`
  and `80` unchanged (`:35`), and returns `+375` when no subscriber digits
  follow (`:39`). `normalizeBelarusPhone` is untouched by the diff, so its
  accept/reject rules are unchanged. The fix report names `npm test -- phone`
  and states 40 of 40 passing, and the new "formatPhoneMask while typing" tests
  cover the typing, paste, idempotency and limitation cases. I did not re-run
  the suite.

  ### New Breakage in the Fix Diff
  None Critical or Important. I traced every case by hand against the final
  code, using text = mask(text + ch) for typing and text =
  mask(text.slice(0,-1)) for deletion.

  (a) Typing `80291234567`:
  - `8` gives `8`, then `80` gives `80`.
  - `802` gives `+375 2`, then `8029` gives `+375 29`.
  - The rest gives `+375 29 1`, `+375 29 12`, `+375 29 123`, `+375 29 123-4`,
  `+375 29 123-45`, `+375 29 123-45-6`, and finally `+375 29 123-45-67`.
  - Nothing clears or garbles.

  (b) Typing `+375291234567`:
  - `+` gives `+`, then `+3`, `+37` and `+375`.
  - `+3752` gives `+375 2`, then `+375 29`.
  - It then follows the same path to `+375 29 123-45-67`.

  (c) Typing `291234567`:
  - `2` gives `+375 2`, then `+375 29`.
  - It then follows the same path to the full mask.

  (d) Deleting back from `+375 29 123-45-67`: the field is never stuck. The mask
  rebuilds from digits only, so a trailing space or hyphen is dropped and the
  next delete lands on a digit.
  - `…-45-6` gives `+375 29 123-45-6`.
  - `…-45-` gives `+375 29 123-45`.
  - `…123-4` gives `+375 29 123-4`.
  - `…123-` gives `+375 29 123`.
  - Then `+375 29 12`, `+375 29 1`, `+375 29` (from `+375 29 `), `+375 2`, and
  `+375` (from `+375 `).
  - Then `+37`, `+3`, `+`, and `` (empty).

  (e) Prefixes 25, 33, 44:
  - `2` gives `+375 2`, then `25` gives `+375 25`. The `44` prefix is handled
  the same way.
  - `3` gives `+375 3`. The next `3` gives digits `37533`, which strips to `33`
  and gives `+375 33`, so it is not misread.
  - Only a bare `375…` typed digit by digit garbles. `3` then `7` reads as code
  `37`, which is the documented limitation, pinned by the test at diff lines
  118-123.

  ### Out-of-Scope Observations
  - Minor, caused by the new code at `lib/phone.ts:35`: typing `+` and then `8`
  gives `8`, which drops the plus. This is harmless, because `+8…` is not a
  valid input anyway.

  ### Verdict
  **Fix round:** All findings addressed, no new Critical/Important breakage

› Message from general-purpose
  ### Spec Compliance
  - ✅ Spec compliant. I checked `lib/lead-schema.ts` against every binding rule
  and found no deviation. I read the diff only and did not run the suite.
  - ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: the zod 4 behavior I rely on, namely `{ error }`
  on `z.string`, `.trim()` before `.optional()`, and `z.NEVER` inside a
  transform. The `error:` option implies zod 4, and the report says the tests
  pass. I did not run `npm test -- lead-schema`.

  Hand-traces for the named risks:
  1. Non-object bodies: `null`, `'x'`, `[]` and `5` all give an invalid_type
  issue with an empty path. That is keyed `body` (lead-schema.ts:130), so
  `errors` is non-empty. `undefined` behaves the same way. A thrown error is
  caught at :134 and returns `{ body: ... }`. Deeply nested objects are not
  walked, since the schema only reads known keys. ✅ Covered by the `it.each`
  test, which asserts `ok:false` and no throw.
  2. Name length: `'a'*60` passes and `'a'*61` fails. Whitespace is trimmed
  before `min` and `max` (:93), so `'   '` fails with the exact message. Length
  counts UTF-16 code units, not code points. Two emoji (4 units) pass. A single
  emoji (2 units) also passes `min(2)`. A name made of astral characters is
  accepted at 1 to 30 characters. That is lenient and harmless, but it is not
  "characters" in the strict sense. The spec does not define it, so I find it
  sensible. ✅ The 60-character boundary is not tested (see Minor).
  3. Phone as number or array: `z.string({ error: PHONE_MSG })` rejects it with
  the exact phone message. The transform does not run, so nothing throws. ✅ Not
  tested (see Minor).
  4. Telegram:
     - `'anna'` (4 characters) fails: the regex needs a letter followed by 4 to
  31 more characters, so 5 to 32 in total.
     - `'@anna_minsk'` gives `'anna_minsk'`.
     - `'@@anna_minsk'` and `'1anna_'` fail.
     - 33 characters fail and 32 pass.
     - Whitespace-only trims to `''`, which returns `''`.
     - ✅ `'@anna_minsk'` is not tested. Neither are the 32 and 33 boundaries or
  whitespace-only.
  5. Attribution:
     - The loop iterates the whitelist (:84) and builds a fresh `{}` (:81). It
  reads a key only if it is an own property.
     - `__proto__` and `constructor` are not whitelisted, so pollution is not
  possible. This is safe by construction.
     - Unknown keys and non-string values are dropped, and values are sliced to
  300 characters.
     - An array, string or null `attribution` returns `{}` with no error. A
  missing one also returns `{}`.
     - The 11-key cap holds because the whitelist has exactly 11 keys.
     - ✅ The array and string cases and the `__proto__` case are not tested
  (see Minor).
  6. idempotencyKey: a space or colon fails the regex, 7 characters fail
  `min(8)`, and 65 fail `max(64)`. 8 characters and a UUID pass. ✅ Only 5
  characters is tested, and not the 8, 64 or 65 boundaries.
  7. Tests: they assert exact strings for the name, phone, comment, telegram and
  interest errors, and exact parsed values for phone, telegram, name,
  attribution and comment. Several cases only assert `toBeDefined()`: name
  length, key, and `renderedAt`.

  ### Strengths
  - The attribution handling is safe against prototype pollution, and the
  whitelist drives the loop.
  - `parseLead` wraps `safeParse` in try/catch, and errors use first-wins keyed
  by field. Unknown top-level keys are stripped.
  - Error strings match the brief exactly. `telegram`, `comment`, `website` and
  `attribution` all default to `''` or `{}` when missing.
  - `data.phone` comes from `normalizeBelarusPhone`.

  ### Issues
  #### Critical (Must Fix)
  - None.
  #### Important (Should Fix)
  - None.
  #### Minor (Nice to Have)
  - tests/lead-schema.test.ts:179-183: no test for the 60-character name
  passing, and the 61-character case only asserts `toBeDefined()`. Assert the
  exact message for the 1-character and 61-character cases and add a passing 60.
  - tests/lead-schema.test.ts:208-222: telegram boundaries are untested (32
  passes, 33 fails, `'1anna_'`, whitespace-only gives `''`). These are the exact
  cases the spec calls out.
  - tests/lead-schema.test.ts:228-232: add 8 and 64 characters passing, 65
  failing, and a UUID passing, and assert the key message string.
  - tests/lead-schema.test.ts:240-250: there is no test for `attribution` as an
  array or string, for `__proto__` or `constructor` keys, or for a missing
  attribution on its own. The pollution safety is real but unguarded against
  regression.
  - tests/lead-schema.test.ts:197-206: no test for a phone given as a number or
  array, the exact case the brief names.
  - lead-schema.ts:98: a non-string `comment`, such as `null`, gets the "no longer than 500" message, which is misleading. It is cosmetic and not specified.

  ### Assessment
  **Task quality:** Approved
  **Reasoning:** The implementation meets every binding rule I could check by
  hand-trace, and it is safe against odd input. The gaps are only missing
  boundary and pollution tests, which are polish and not behavioral defects.

● Agent "Review Task 8a header and hero" finished · 1m 12s

› Message from general-purpose
  ### Spec Compliance
  - ✅ Spec compliant. Every dispatch decision is implemented.
  `StoredLead`/`LeadStore` have the specified shapes. The handler order is 400,
  honeypot, fast-fill, rate limit, claim, save with release, notify. All
  messages and statuses match the brief. The route covers 413, malformed JSON,
  IP extraction, the named `notifyLead`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, and has no
  segment config. Every required test case is present in
  tests/handle-lead.test.ts and tests/lead-route.test.ts.
  - ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff: the Upstash path is never run against real
  Redis. I traced it by hand against @upstash/redis 1.39.0 in node_modules
  (findings below).

  ### Strengths
  - The claim uses `SET NX EX`, which is atomic. With two concurrent submits,
  different keys and the same phone, exactly one wins and the loser reads the
  winner's leadId from the GET (risk 2). A Redis error in set/get is not caught
  in store.ts:31-45, so it reaches handleLead's catch and returns 503 instead of
  being treated as "newly claimed" (risk 1).
  - `releasePhone` is only reachable after `existing === null`, meaning only
  after this request took the claim. A duplicate can never release the original
  lead's claim (risk 4).
  - Fast-fill compares only the client's `submittedAt` with `renderedAt`. A
  missing value or NaN skips the check. The server clock is never used
  (handle-lead.ts:42-49).
  - The route takes the first `x-forwarded-for` entry, not the last
  (route.ts:16). A non-object JSON body (`[]`, `5`, `null`) goes to zod and gets
  a 400. `parseLead` also wraps everything in try/catch, so there is no 500
  (risk 8).
  - The concurrency test really races. Both `handleLead` calls start inside the
  `Promise.all` array before either is awaited, and they interleave at the store
  awaits (risk 9).

  ### Issues
  #### Critical (Must Fix)
  None.

  #### Important (Should Fix)
  1. **A lead can be lost while the user is told it succeeded: the same key's
  own claim counts as a duplicate.** lib/handle-lead.ts:67-69 returns
  `duplicate:true` whenever `existing !== null`, including when `existing ===
  lead.idempotencyKey`. There are three ways in:
     - **(a) Lost response, SDK retry.** @upstash/redis retries on fetch failure
  (chunk-4WQYU7T6.mjs:167-191, 5 attempts). Suppose the first `SET NX` reaches
  Redis but its response is lost. The retry returns null, the GET returns this
  request's own key, and `claimPhone` returns it. The handler then answers 200
  duplicate, never calls `saveLead`, and never notifies.
     - **(b) Release fails.** `saveLead` throws and `releasePhone` also throws
  (the error is swallowed at :86-90). The user presses «Retry» with the same
  key and gets duplicate:true. Nothing is saved or notified.
     - **(c) Function dies between claim and save.** The function times out or
  is killed after the claim and before the save. The client retries and gets the
  same result as (b).

     Fix: when `existing === lead.idempotencyKey`, continue to save. `saveLead`
  is already NX-idempotent.
  2. **A failed EXPIRE can block an IP permanently.** In the rate-limit counter
  (lib/store.ts:58-63), `INCR` and `EXPIRE` are separate HTTP calls, and EXPIRE
  runs only when `count === 1`. Any transient error on that EXPIRE (not just
  process death) leaves `rl:<ip>` without a TTL. handleLead fails open for that
  one request (:51-56), but the next hits see count > 1 and never set a TTL.
  From the 6th hit on, that IP gets 429 forever. Office or mobile CGNAT IPs are
  shared, so many real users could be blocked. Fix: use a `multi()`/pipeline for
  INCR + EXPIRE, or call `expire(key, 600, 'NX')` on every hit.
  3. **Personal data leaks into logs.** `UpstashError`'s message embeds the full
  command: `${body2.error}, command was: ${JSON.stringify(req.body)}`
  (node_modules/@upstash/redis/chunk-4WQYU7T6.mjs:205). So `console.error('lead:
  save failed', error)` (handle-lead.ts:85) writes the whole lead JSON to
  Vercel logs whenever Redis returns an error: name, phone, telegram, comment,
  attribution. The claim and release logs (:64, :89) write the phone. Fix: log
  only `error.name` plus a redacted message, or strip the text after "command
  was:".

  #### Minor (Nice to Have)
  1. **A replay after the claim expires notifies twice.** If the same key comes
  back after the claim expired, `saveLead`'s `SET NX` is a no-op, but handleLead
  still calls `notify` (handle-lead.ts:93-97). The manager gets a second
  Telegram message for a lead that is already stored. The test at
  tests/handle-lead.test.ts:186-194 does not assert the notify count. `saveLead`
  returning void hides whether the lead was new.
  2. **An orphaned lead is never added back to the list.** Suppose `SET
  lead:<id> NX` succeeds and the `LPUSH` is lost (crash, or an SDK retry of the
  SET gets null). The lead record exists but is never in `leads`, and a retry
  with the same key skips the LPUSH again (store.ts:52-56). The data is still
  findable by scanning `lead:*`, and the notification is still sent on the
  error/retry path, so this is Minor as you framed it. One option is `LPUSH`
  inside a `multi` with the SET, or `LPOS`/`SADD` repair on the not-new path.
  3. **A double expiry is reported as a duplicate.** The claim retry branch
  (store.ts:39-43) returns the caller's own `leadId` when the key expires twice
  in a row. That gives "duplicate of self", which feeds Important #1. Returning
  null, or throwing, is safer.
  4. **Release does not check ownership.** `releasePhone` runs an unconditional
  `DEL` (store.ts:47-49). If a slow save outlives the 600 s claim and another
  lead claims the phone, the release deletes that other lead's claim. This is
  very unlikely. A compare-and-delete fixes it.
  5. **The fake success is distinguishable by timing.** Its body shape matches a
  real success, `{ok:true, leadId}` with no `duplicate` field, and echoing the
  client's own key is harmless. But it returns without any Redis round-trips, so
  a bot measuring latency could tell it apart (risk 6).
  6. **Some tests are weak.** The concurrency test
  (tests/handle-lead.test.ts:196-207) never asserts that one response is
  `{ok:true, leadId:'key-0001', duplicate:true}`. The fast-fill test checks only
  the status, not the exact body, and the 429 test does not check `message`.
  7. **The whole body is read before the size check.** route.ts:22 buffers the
  full body before checking its length, so there is no streaming cap. Vercel's
  4.5 MB platform limit bounds this.
  8. **A new Redis client is created per request.** `createUpstashStore()` runs
  on every request (route.ts:36). Hoisting a module-level lazy store would reuse
  the client.

  ### Assessment
  **Task quality:** Needs fixes
  **Reasoning:** The handler order, the claim's atomicity and the tests match

  1024px) * 0.1923), 120px)`. Padding and resulting content width at each

  | Viewport | Padding | Content width |
  |---|---|---|
  | 1024 | 40 | 944 |

  | 1600 | clamped to 120 | 1200 (container capped at maxWidth 1440, centred) |

  - At 1440 the content is exactly 1200 with no extra gutter.

  over the fixed `bag-body`.

✻ Sautéed for 38s · done 20:41

› Message from general-purpose
  ### Finding Verdicts

  - **Own key's claim counted as a duplicate, so a retry lost the lead (finding
  1)**: ADDRESSED for the claim path. There is a related hole in `saveLead`,
  described as N1 below. The fix is at lib/handle-lead.ts:67, `if (existing !==
  null && existing !== lead.idempotencyKey)`. `saveLead` now returns a boolean
  (lib/store.ts:205-212), and the handler notifies only when it gets `true`
  (handle-lead.ts:96-101).
    - **(a) Lost SET response, SDK retries.** SET phone:P K NX EX 600 lands in
  Redis, but the response is lost. The SDK retry gets null, the GET returns K,
  and claimPhone returns K. Since K equals the request's own key, the handler
  continues. `saveLead`: SET lead:K NX gives OK, then LPUSH leads K, and it
  returns true. notify runs once and the user gets 200 {ok, leadId:K}.
    - **(b) Save and release both throw.** The first request fails on
  `saveLead`, `releasePhone` also throws (logged), and the user gets 503.
  phone:P=K remains. On retry with K the claim returns K (own key), so the
  handler continues. `saveLead` returns true, notify runs once, and the user
  gets 200. Test at tests/handle-lead.test.ts:228-252.
    - **(c) Function dies between claim and save.** On retry the claim returns
  K, `saveLead` returns true, notify runs once, and the user gets 200. Same path
  as (b).
    - All three are "exactly once" only if the earlier failure happened before
  SET lead:K reached Redis. If that SET landed, see N1.
    - **R2 with a different key (K2, same phone, within 600 s).** SET NX fails
  and the GET returns K1, which is not K2. The user gets 200 {ok, leadId:K1,
  duplicate:true}, with no save and no notify. Covered by the existing test at
  :48-59.
    - **R1 replayed with K1 (inside or after the window).** The claim returns K1
  (own key) or null after expiry. SET lead:K1 NX is not OK, so `saveLead`
  returns false and nothing is pushed or notified. The user gets 200 {ok,
  leadId:K1} without the duplicate flag. There is no second lead and no second
  notification. Tests at :188-196 and :254-262.
    - **Concurrent, different keys.** One SET NX on phone:P wins. The loser's
  GET returns the winner's key, so the loser gets 200 duplicate:true with the
  winner's leadId. Result: one lead, one notify.
    - **Concurrent, same key.** Both requests take the own-key path. SET lead:K
  NX succeeds for only one of them, so that one notifies and the other's
  `saveLead` returns false. Both get 200 {ok, leadId:K}. Result: one lead, one
  notify. The test at :203-213 asserts exact bodies.
    - If the key expires between SET and GET twice in a row, the handler now
  throws and answers 503 (store.ts:195).

  - **A failed EXPIRE could leave a rate-limit key with no TTL and block an IP
  forever (finding 2)**: ADDRESSED. The new code is store.ts:220,
  `r.multi().incr(key).expire(key, 600, 'NX').exec<[number, number]>()`.
    - The API usage matches @upstash/redis 1.39.0. `multi: () => Pipeline<[]>`
  is at error-DOszGRxg.d.ts:4796, `Pipeline.expire(...CommandArgs<typeof
  ExpireCommand>)` at :3679, and the ExpireCommand option `ExpireOption = "NX" |
  ...` at :1488-1490.
    - The multi is sent as one atomic multi-exec request. The count comes from
  index 0, the INCR result, which is correct.
    - Any per-command error makes `exec` throw an UpstashError
  (chunk-4WQYU7T6.mjs:3500). The existing catch at handle-lead.ts:54-56 handles
  it, so the request still fails open.
    - Counter semantics are unchanged: the window starts at the first hit,
  EXPIRE NX never extends it, and the 6th request gets 429 (test at :285-300
  asserts the exact message). EXPIRE NX on every hit also heals any old key left
  without a TTL.
    - Tests: tests/store.test.ts:27-33 asserts the Upstash call shape (incr,
  then expire with 600 and 'NX' inside `multi`), but only for one hit. The
  memory store sets expiresAt on every new window, and :35-43 covers that. There
  is no Upstash test for a second hit or for the count read from `exec`. That
  gap is Minor.

  - **Personal data leaked into logs through UpstashError messages (finding
  3)**: ADDRESSED in the code. The regression test is weak (N2).
    - I checked every console call in lib/ and app/. There are exactly five:
  handle-lead.ts:55, :64, :86, :90 and :99. store.ts and route.ts have none.
    - Each call passes only a constant string, the leadId (the client-generated
  idempotencyKey) and `safeErrorLabel(...)`, which returns a string. No raw
  error object is passed, so `stack` and `cause` are never printed.
    - `safeErrorLabel` (store.ts:172-176) uses only `name` and `message` cut at
  'command was:', capped at 200 characters. Non-Error values become
  'UnknownError'.
    - The other Upstash error messages carry no lead data. The pipeline error
  says "Command N [ INCR ] failed: …", with no arguments. UpstashJSONParseError
  holds the server's response body, not the request.
    - handleLead has no uncaught path where Next.js could log a raw stack.

  ### New Breakage in the Fix Diff

  - **N1, Important: lib/store.ts:205-212 together with handle-lead.ts:96.** A
  lead can be stored but never listed and never notified, while the user is told
  it succeeded.
    - `saveLead` runs SET lead:K NX and LPUSH as two separate HTTP calls. The
  fix now ties notification to `created`, which `saveLead` returns as true only
  when SET returned OK.
    - **LPUSH fails after SET succeeded.** The first attempt throws, the claim
  is released, and the user gets 503. On retry with K: the claim succeeds, SET
  NX is not OK, `saveLead` returns false, and no LPUSH or notify happens. The
  user gets 200, but the lead is not in the `leads` list and the owner is never
  told.
    - **SDK lost-response retry on SET lead:K.** This is the same mechanism as
  finding 1(a), now one step later. The retry returns null and `saveLead`
  returns false: no LPUSH, no notify, 200.
    - **Function dies between SET and LPUSH or notify.** Same outcome.
    - Before this fix, notify was unconditional, so the owner still heard about
  the lead. This is a regression in the exact "lost lead" class.
    - Suggested fix: decide "already done" by a completion marker rather than by
  SET NX alone. One option is a Lua script that does SET NX plus LPUSH
  atomically. Another is a `notified:K` key (SET NX after notify, or LPUSH
  guarded by an LPOS check), so a retry with the same key finishes the missing
  steps. This gives at-least-once notification.

  - **N2, Minor: tests/handle-lead.test.ts:378-398.** The PII test cannot catch
  the regression it targets. `JSON.stringify` of an Error gives `{}` because
  `message` and `stack` are not enumerable. I confirmed this with node:
  `[["lead: save failed",{}]]`. If someone logged the raw error again, the test
  would still pass. Fix: map each logged argument through `util.inspect` (or
  `String(a)` plus `a?.stack`) before checking.

  - **C, Minor (deferred, still open): handle-lead.ts:89 → store.ts:201-203.**
  `releasePhone` runs an unconditional DEL. If K1's save stalls longer than 600
  s, the claim expires and K2 claims P. When K1's save then fails, the DEL
  removes K2's claim, and a K3 inside the window would save a duplicate lead.
  This produces an extra lead, not a lost one, and needs a stall over 10
  minutes, which is unlikely within Vercel's limits. Fix: compare-and-delete
  (only DEL if the value is K), via Lua.

  - **Route singleton (route.ts:29, :53): no breakage.** `createUpstashStore`
  does not touch the environment until first use (`Redis.fromEnv` is lazy inside
  `client()`), so it cannot throw at creation. Failures surface inside the
  store methods and are already handled. tests/lead-route.test.ts mocks
  `createUpstashStore` and returns a memory store. Its two tests never reach the
  store, so the shared singleton has no effect on them.

  - **Changed `LeadStore` signature, `saveLead` returning `Promise<boolean>`: no
  breakage.** All test stubs delegate to `store.saveLead`, so none returns
  `undefined` and silently skips notify. `let created: boolean` is definitely
  assigned, because the catch returns.

  - **Edited test expectations: all three got stricter, none weaker.**
    - Fast-fill (:273): status-only became the exact body.
    - 429 (:294-297): `ok:false` became the exact message.
    - Concurrent double submit (:321-323): status-only became exact bodies. The
  loser has no `duplicate` flag. That is correct under the own-key ruling, and
  the brief never required `duplicate:true` for a same-key loser.

  ### Out-of-Scope Observations

  - EXPIRE NX needs Redis 7 or later. If the Upstash database ever rejects it,
  every `hitRate` call throws, and rate limiting is silently turned off
  (fail-open, logged on each request). A staging check, or a log alert on 'rate
  limit unavailable', would catch this.
  - This behavior predates the fix: a resubmit that reuses key K with a
  different phone saves nothing new, because SET lead:K NX is false, yet still
  answers 200. That holds only if the client keeps the key after the user edits
  the form.

  ### Verdict

  **Fix round:** Findings 1, 2 and 3 are addressed in the code, but the fix adds
  new Important breakage. N1 is the problem: the non-atomic SET + LPUSH in
  `saveLead`, combined with notify-only-when-created, can make a stored lead
  invisible and unnotified while the user gets 200. N1 should be fixed before
  this round is accepted. N2 and C are Minor.

● The Task 5 re-review confirmed all three original findings are fixed. But it
  found a new Important problem that comes from my own ruling to notify only
  when the save created the lead. If SET succeeds and the follow-up LPUSH is
  lost, the lead is stored but never listed or notified, while the user is told
  it succeeded. That's the same "lost lead" class as before, so this needs a
  second fix round. My ruling for it: make the save atomic with a Lua script,
  and track notification with its own claimable marker, so a retry can finish
  the missing steps without double-notifying.

› Message from general-purpose
  ### Finding Verdicts

  The fix commit cdbbea5 is checked out in worktree
  `/home/usr/projects/coffee/.claude/worktrees/agent-a2efa5f83de3e2573`. Paths
  below are relative to it. Nothing in the tree was changed. I ran the two Lua
  scripts against a real Redis 7.2.9 in a throwaway `alpine:3.20` container. The
  script was at scratchpad `check.sh`. Results: save script 1 then 0, `LLEN
  leads`=1, first value kept. Release with the wrong owner returns 0 and the key
  stays. Release with the right owner returns 1 and the key is deleted. Release
  on a missing key returns 0.

  - **N1: a lead could be stored but never listed or notified, while the user
  got 200.** ADDRESSED.
    - `lib/store.ts:31-36,77-83`: one EVAL does `SET lead:<id> NX` and then
  `LPUSH leads <id>` only if the SET happened.
    - `lib/handle-lead.ts:94-112`: after every successful `saveLead`, new or
  replay, it calls `claimNotification`. It notifies when the claim succeeds, or
  when the claim call throws. If notify throws, it calls `releaseNotification`.
  The marker is set at `store.ts:86` (`SET notify:<id> 1 NX EX 604800`).
    - Traces:
      - (i) The eval throws before running: nothing is written. Then
  `releasePhone(P,K)` deletes our own claim and the user gets 503. Retry with K:
  claimPhone returns null, eval returns 1 (stored and listed once),
  claimNotification is OK, one notify, 200. The SET-without-LPUSH split no
  longer exists, because an EVAL is atomic. Only two failures are left: eval not
  applied, and eval applied but the response lost.
      - (ii) Lost response: the script's write landed (stored and listed), the
  client throws, the claim is released (own key), the user gets 503. Retry with
  K: claimPhone returns null, eval returns 0 (no-op), the marker is free so OK,
  one notify, 200. Stored once, listed once, notified once. Covered by
  `tests/handle-lead.test.ts:272` and by `tests/store.test.ts` through the
  Upstash mock.
      - (iii) The function dies after claimPhone, before saveLead: `phone:P`
  holds K for up to 600 s and there is no lead. Retry with K: claimPhone returns
  K, which is our own key, so it continues: eval returns 1, notify, 200. The
  trace is correct; the different-key case is under Out-of-Scope.
      - (iv) The function dies after the marker is claimed, before notify: the
  lead is stored and listed, but the notification is lost. The `notify:K` marker
  lives 7 days, so every replay in that time skips notify. This is the accepted
  cost of claiming the marker before notifying.
      - (v) Notifier throws: the marker is released and the user still gets 200.
  A replay with K: eval returns 0, the marker is OK, notify runs. A third
  replay skips. Covered at `tests/handle-lead.test.ts:177`.
      - (vi) Concurrent requests with the same key: A's claimPhone returns null;
  B's returns K, its own key, so it continues. Both run the atomic eval: one
  gets 1, the other 0. Both call claimNotification: exactly one gets OK, so
  exactly one notify. Two notifications happen only if claimNotification throws
  on a request (it then notifies anyway), which the ruling accepts. Covered at
  `tests/handle-lead.test.ts:224`, which now also checks `leadIds()`.
      - (vii) Replay long after success: after 600 s the phone claim has
  expired, claimPhone returns null, eval returns 0, the marker is held so notify
  is skipped, 200. Covered at `:213`. A replay after the 7-day marker TTL would
  notify a second time; see Out-of-Scope.
    - Lua and the Upstash client, read and checked live:
      - In Redis Lua, a SET NX on a free key gives the status table `{ok='OK'}`,
  which is truthy. On an existing key it gives a nil bulk, which becomes
  `false`. So the `if` test is correct.
      - LPUSH runs only inside the success branch.
      - Return values are 1 or 0 and are ignored by the caller.
      - KEYS[1]=`lead:<id>`, KEYS[2]=`leads`, ARGV[1]=JSON, ARGV[2]=id. This
  matches the call at `store.ts:79-82` and the shape test.
      - Every key is passed in KEYS; no key name is built inside the script.
      - `@upstash/redis` 1.39.0 has `eval(script, keys: string[], args: TArgs)`,
  sent as `["eval", script, keys.length, ...keys, ...args]` (chunk:1428-1431).
  `defaultSerializer` passes strings through unchanged. The arguments are in
  that order and both values are strings.

  - **N2: the PII test could not catch a raw Error being logged.** ADDRESSED.
    - `tests/handle-lead.test.ts`: the `dump` helper runs every logged argument
  through `inspect(a, {depth:10, showHidden:true})`.
    - A probe logs a raw `new Error(command)` and asserts the dump contains
  `+375291234567`, so the check is not vacuous.
    - Both `saveLead` and `releasePhone` throw messages carrying the phone and
  name. The test then asserts neither the phone nor `Anna` appears.
    - I grepped every `console.*` call in `lib/` and `app/`. They are all in
  `lib/handle-lead.ts:55,64,85,89,99,105,109`. Each logs only a fixed string,
  the idempotency key or leadId, and `safeErrorLabel(...)`. None logs a raw
  error, phone, name, telegram, comment or attribution. `app/api/lead/route.ts`
  has no console calls.

  - **C: releasePhone deleted the claim unconditionally.** ADDRESSED.
    - `store.ts:38-42,73-75`: compare-and-delete in Lua (`GET == ARGV[1]` then
  `DEL`).
    - Memory store, `store.ts:124`: deletes only when the stored leadId matches.
    - The only call site is `handle-lead.ts:87`, inside the `saveLead` catch,
  with this request's own `lead.idempotencyKey`. The claim value is that same
  key (`:62`).
    - 600 s stall trace: K1 claims P, then its save stalls. The claim expires
  and K2 claims `phone:P=K2`. K1's save fails, so it runs release(P,K1): GET
  returns K2, not K1, so it returns 0 and nothing is deleted. K3 inside the
  window gets K2 back and is answered as a duplicate (200, `duplicate:true`),
  without saving a second lead.
    - Confirmed live, and by `tests/handle-lead.test.ts:297` against the memory
  store.

  ### New Breakage in the Fix Diff
  None at Critical or Important.

  - Changed test expectations:
    - Every edited test asserts more than before. The flaky-save test now checks
  that the first attempt does not notify and the retry notifies exactly once.
  The notifier-throws test checks replay behavior. The concurrent test checks
  the list. The expiry-replay test now holds the "no second notify" assertion
  that used to be its own test.
    - Nothing was loosened.
    - The hand-built store wrappers became `{...store, ...}`. That is safe
  because the memory store's methods are closures and do not use `this`.
  - Interface consumers: `route.ts` only passes the store through to
  `handleLead`, and it compiles against the new interface. No other callers of
  `saveLead`'s old boolean result remain.
  - The response to the user does not change when Redis marker calls fail:
    - claimNotification throws: it is logged, notify runs, the user gets 200.
    - releaseNotification throws: it is logged, the user gets 200.
  - Minor (test fidelity, review item C): the Upstash `eval` mock in
  `tests/store.test.ts` picks its behavior with `script.includes('LPUSH')` and
  then re-implements the intended logic using `keys`/`args`. It does not depend
  on what the script says.
    - What the tests do check: the order of keys and args at the call site, and
  that the script text contains `'NX'` and `LPUSH`.
    - What they do not check: a wrong `KEYS`/`ARGV` index inside the script, or
  an inverted `if`.
    - I closed that gap for this review by hand with the live Redis run above.
  Upstash's own Lua runtime was not exercised.
    - Smallest durable check: a test that runs only when `REDIS_URL` is set. It
  would run both scripts twice through `redis-cli` or ioredis against a
  disposable Redis, mirroring my check. Or run a one-off `redis.eval` against an
  Upstash dev database before launch.

  ### Out-of-Scope Observations
  - At-least-once notification relies on the client replaying the request.
    - If the first notify throws, the user still gets 200, so the client will
  not replay. The notification is then lost even though the lead is stored and
  listed. The same happens when a save's response is lost and the user does not
  retry after the 503.
    - This follows from the contract ("notification failure never changes the
  response"). Task 6's notifier should therefore retry internally, or the owner
  should review the `leads` list.
  - A replay of the same key more than 7 days later re-notifies, because the
  `notify:` TTL has expired by then. This is harmless and unlikely.
  - Redis does not roll back a script when it fails partway. If `LPUSH` failed
  after `SET` (for example, `leads` is not a list because of an operator error,
  or a memory-limit edge case), the record would stay stored but unlisted, and
  replays would no-op. This is operational only.
  - Already in the design: if the function dies between claimPhone and saveLead,
  and the client then submits the same phone with a NEW key within 600 s, the
  user gets `duplicate:true` pointing to a lead that does not exist. It is worth
  confirming that the client keeps one idempotency key across retries and
  reloads.

  ### Verdict
  **Fix round:** All findings addressed, no new Critical/Important breakage

  real Redis 7.2 instance, which settles my main concern about the mock-only
  testing. I'll log the new constraints it surfaced for Tasks 6 and 9, then send

  ### Strengths
  - The FAQ is server-only, with semantic details/summary and no JS. A CSS-only
  marker replaces the default one, and the `-webkit-details-marker`, `::marker`

  ### Issues
  #### Critical (Must Fix)
  None.

  #### Important (Should Fix)
  None.

  #### Minor (Nice to Have)
  - The FAQ answers carry `role="region"` plus `aria-labelledby`. This is valid.
  A closed details body is not rendered, so only the open items are exposed. If
  a user opens all four, that creates four region landmarks, which is mild
  noise. Dropping the role on the answers would be simpler, since `details`
  already conveys the relationship.
  - The plus/minus indicator is set through `content: "+"` and `content:
  "\2212"` with no alt text. Some screen readers will read "plus" or "minus"
  after the question. `content: "+" / ""` would silence it, with the plain
  `content` as a fallback.
  - Heading outline: the footer has three `h2` column titles (Address, Contact, Delivery) with no parent footer heading. The outline is still sane, and the h2
  choice is acceptable. A visually hidden `h2` "Contacts" with `h3` column
  titles would be slightly cleaner, but it is not needed.
  - The Telegram and Instagram links open in the same tab and have no
  `target="_blank"` or `rel`. The brief only requires `rel="noopener
  noreferrer"` where `target="_blank"` is intended, so this is acceptable. If
  you want them in a new tab, add both attributes.
  - The footer pickup hours are a hardcoded string. If hours live elsewhere
  (`lib/schedule.ts` or the delivery section), they could drift. I did not check
  where they are defined.
  - The footer has 3 columns, not the brief's "four". The brief itself lists
  only three, and the report flags this. At `sm` the 2-column grid leaves the
  third column alone on the second row, which is cosmetic.
  - The fictional-project line is 12px. That is acceptable for "small text", and
  it is 9.1:1 against ink.

  **Reasoning:** The copy, contrast, tap targets, motion, layout and privacy
  metadata all meet the brief, and no token, `'use client'` or extra-file
  violations turned up. Only accessibility-polish items remain, all Minor.

       - Thu 15 Oct 10:00: `nextRoast` is 22 Oct and the cutoff is Wed 21 Oct,

  is about 52–60px.

✻ Crunched for 8s · done 20:52

› Message from general-purpose
  ### Spec Compliance
  - ❌ Issues found:
    - **The truncation removes text instead of shortening it, and drops the
  "…".** The spec says to shorten the raw comment and add "…", then shorten the
  attribution values. `formatLeadMessage` (lib/telegram.ts:119-134) cuts
  `commentMax` by the *escaped* overflow (`message.length - 3800`) but counts
  that amount in *raw* characters. Escaping makes a character up to 6 times
  longer (`"` becomes `&quot;`), so the first step usually overshoots to 0. When
  a value is clipped to 0 its line disappears completely, with no "…".
      - Input: 10 attribution values of 300 `v` plus a comment of `'Q"'×250`.
  Result: 3230 chars, and the "Comment" line is **gone**. Cutting about 290
  raw chars would have kept about 210 chars of the comment.
      - Your worst case (comment `'<'×500`, 11 values of `'&'×300`, name
  `'&'×60`): the result is **524 chars**. The comment and every attribution line
  are dropped, although about 3300 chars of budget were left.
      - Tags and entities do stay intact and the length stays ≤ 3800. The bug is
  lost data, not broken HTML.
  - Everything else matches the dispatch decisions:
    - Exports and signatures are as specified. `sendTelegram`'s extra `sleep`
  parameter has a default, so `notify(stored)` and `notifyLead = sendTelegram`
  still type-check.
    - Every user value is HTML-escaped (`& < > " '`). The title is in the right
  format. Empty lines are left out. The phone is plain text. The 3000 ms timeout
  per attempt, 2 attempts and 400 ms pause are as specified. 429 is retried
  only when `retry_after ≤ 1`. Other 4xx are not retried. It throws `telegram
  send failed: <status|error.name>[ description≤120]`.
    - Env vars are read at call time, and an empty string counts as missing.
  There is one warn per process and it contains no values.
    - The route change is minimal: only the binding changed and the now-unused
  `StoredLead` import was removed.
    - `.env.example` is unchanged. The diff has no `bot<digits>:` pattern and no
  `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN=<value>`. Only the three expected files changed.
  - ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: whether Vercel's `maxDuration` and plan limits
  allow the 6.4 s worst case added to the Upstash calls. The route sets no
  `maxDuration`.

  ### Strengths
  I ran the module's code with `node --experimental-strip-types`. Inputs and
  results:

  **Secret leakage (risk 1)**
  - Every path into the error message uses either the HTTP status or
  `error.name`. `String(err)`, `err.message`, `err.cause` and `err.stack` of the
  fetch error are never used.
  - The thrown `Error` has no `cause`. I confirmed `'cause' in e === false`.
  - `DOMException instanceof Error` is true in Node 22.21. A real timeout
  therefore gives `telegram send failed: TimeoutError`.
  - A non-JSON 502 body gives `telegram send failed: 502`.
  - The only console output is the fixed warn string.
  - In handleLead, `safeErrorLabel` wraps this as `Error: telegram send failed:
  …`. That is 22 + status + 120 chars, so it fits under its 200-char cap. No
  token, URL, text, phone, name or comment can reach it.

  **HTML injection (risk 2)**
  - Name `<b>x</b> & "q" '` becomes `&lt;b&gt;x&lt;/b&gt; &amp; &quot;q&quot;
  &#39;`.
  - Comment `</a><a href="javascript:alert(1)">` comes out fully escaped.
  - Nick `anna_minsk` becomes `<a
  href="https://t.me/anna_minsk">@anna_minsk</a>`, and the nick is escaped as
  well.
  - `landing` and `referrer` are plain escaped text, never links.
  `https://evil.example/<x>&` becomes `https://evil.example/&lt;x&gt;&amp;`.

  **Retry logic and timing (risk 4)**
  | Case | Result |
  |---|---|
  | 500, then 200 | 2 calls, `sleep(400)`, resolves |
  | 400 | 1 call, throws |
  | 429 with retry_after 1 | `sleep(1000)`, then retried |
  | 429 with retry_after 30 | 1 call, throws |
  | TypeError, then 200 | 2 calls, resolves |
  | Hanging fetch (real timers) | 2 calls, throws `TimeoutError` after **6403
  ms** |

  **Time format and tests**
  - `formatMinskTime`: `2026-10-14T22:30Z` gives `15.10.2026 01:30`, and
  `2026-12-31T21:30Z` gives `01.01.2027 00:30`. Values are zero-padded and only
  `getUTC*` getters are used.
  - Tests check exact strings and the exact request body. Sleep is injected, so
  the suite is fast.
  - The leak test throws a TypeError whose message contains the URL and token
  and whose `cause` holds the token. It then checks `err.message`, `err.stack`
  and every console call through `inspect(…, {showHidden:true})`.
  - No earlier test expectation was edited.

  ### Issues
  #### Critical (Must Fix)
  None.

  #### Important (Should Fix)
  1. **Truncation overshoot drops the comment and attribution entirely**
  (lib/telegram.ts:124-132). The inputs and results are in Spec Compliance
  above. A manager can lose the customer's comment with no "…" marker, even when
  the budget had room.
     - Smallest fix: binary-search the largest `commentMax` in `[0, commentLen]`
  whose render fits. If even `commentMax = 0` is too long, binary-search
  `attrMax` the same way. That is about 9 renders each.
     - Add to the worst-case test that the "Comment" line is still there and
  ends with `…` when attribution alone fits, and that `message.length > 3800 -
  ~10`. Today the test only checks the upper bound, which is why it passed.

  #### Minor (Nice to Have)
  1. **Telegram's `description` goes into the logs.** It is remote-controlled
  text, capped at 120 chars, and handleLead logs it.
     - I know of no sendMessage error whose description repeats the user's text.
  The parse-entities errors name only tag names and byte offsets. Since every
  user value is escaped, the only tags Telegram can see are ours, so the PII
  risk is low.
     - Defence in depth: put in the thrown message only the status plus the
  description's text before its first `:`, e.g. `Bad Request`, `Forbidden`, `Too
  Many Requests`. Or remove quoted fragments.
  2. **User-visible latency on a Telegram outage.** handleLead awaits notify
  before it answers. The worst case adds 3 + 0.4 + 3 = **6.4 s**, or about 6.9 s
  with a slow 429 and `retry_after = 1`.
     - That is under a 10 s limit, but tight once the 3–4 Upstash calls are
  added, and the user stares at a spinner even though the lead is already saved.
     - Suggestion: one shared deadline of about 4 s, using
  `AbortSignal.timeout(min(3000, deadline - now))` and skipping the retry when
  less than about 1 s is left. Or add `export const maxDuration` to the route.
  3. **Test gaps:**
     - No timeout (DOMException `TimeoutError`) test and no
  network-error-then-success test. The code handles both; I verified them by
  hand.
     - `assertSafe` does not run `inspect(err, {showHidden:true})` on the error
  itself, so a future `cause:` would go unnoticed.
     - No check that the leak assertion can fail, e.g. that `assertSafe(new
  Error(TOKEN))` throws.
  4. `formatMinskTime('garbage')` returns `"NaN.NaN.NaN NaN:NaN"`. It does not
  throw, and `createdAt` is created on the server, so this is harmless. A
  fallback to the raw string would read better.
  5. The body of a successful response is never consumed. A `res.body?.cancel()`
  would release the socket immediately. This is a nit.

  ### Assessment
  **Task quality:** Needs fixes
  **Reasoning:** Secret and PII handling, escaping, retry rules and env handling
  are correct and verified by tracing. But truncation shrinks by escaped length
  while counting raw characters, so the comment and attribution can be deleted
  outright instead of shortened with "…". That is a missed requirement that
  causes data loss in the manager's message.
● The Task 6 review found one Important defect: the truncation counts escaped
  overflow in raw characters, so on a long comment it can delete the comment and
  attribution lines outright instead of shortening them. The manager would lose
  the customer's message. I'm sending that back with a binary-search fix. I'm
  also folding in the latency point, because a Telegram outage would currently
  add up to 6.4 s of spinner for a customer whose lead is already saved. A
  shared deadline of about 4 s fixes that.

  ### Finding Verdicts
  - **Finding 1: truncation overshoot drops the comment and attribution
  (lib/telegram.ts:96-118)** — ADDRESSED. Escaped overflow is no longer used as
  a raw cut. `largest()` (l.86-94) binary-searches the largest `commentMax`
  whose render fits, then falls back to `attrMax`, with the comment at 0. I ran
  `formatLeadMessage` on the cases below (the "line" column is the Comment
  line).

  | Case | Length | Comment line | Tags / `&` / surrogates |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | (i) 10 attr values of 300 `v`, comment `'Q"'`x250 | 3799 (was 3230) |
  present, 575 chars, ends `…` | tags balanced, no dangling `&`, no lone
  surrogate |
  | (ii) comment `'<'`x500, 11 values of 300 `&`, name `'&'`x60 | 3779 (was 524)
  | missing | tags balanced, no dangling `&`, no lone surrogate |
  | (iii) 500 Cyrillic chars with quotes and `&`, 8 UTM values of about 60 chars
  | 1306 | fully kept, no truncation | tags balanced, no dangling `&`, no lone
  surrogate |
  | (iv) comment 😀x500, 10 values of 300 `v` | 3800 | present, 576 chars, ends
  `…` | tags balanced, no dangling `&`, no lone surrogate |
  | (iv) odd comment `'a😀'`x900, 10 values of 330 `v` | 3800 | present, ends
  `…` | tags balanced, no dangling `&`, no lone surrogate |
  | (iv) comment 😀x500 plus 11 values of 😀x300 | 3793 | missing | tags
  balanced, no dangling `&`, no lone surrogate |

    - **Case (ii), comment dropped:** the implementer's policy is dropping the
  comment entirely while attribution shrinks to fit. I'm rating this Minor. I
  could not make the comment disappear with realistic input.
      - Unescaped 500-char Cyrillic comment plus 11 values of `a&b` x33 (about
  100 chars) gives 3047 chars with the comment intact.
      - The comment only vanished when every one of 11 attribution values was
  escape-heavy (`"` x100 or `&` x100), or 11 values of 😀x300.
      - It would be more customer-friendly to keep some comment and trim
  attribution harder, but the spec order (comment first, then attribution)
  permits the current behaviour.
    - **Worst-case test assertions:** the worst-case test did not get the
  "Comment line present and ends with …" assertion the finding asked for.
  That would fail by design, because the comment is dropped in that input. The
  new test "keeps as much comment as fits when escaping inflates it" covers case
  (i) instead. It asserts length ≤ 3800, > 3750, and a loose `/&quot;…$|Q…$/`
  match on the comment line.
    - **Item 1 test coverage:** the worst-case test's `> 3790` bound only checks
  the fit-to-cap behaviour for attribution shrinking, not the comment.
  - **(a) Shared 4000 ms deadline** — ADDRESSED. Constants are at l.6-9 and the
  loop at l.193-230. I injected `now`/`sleep` (sleep advances the clock):

  | Scenario | fetch calls | Per-attempt timeouts | Sleeps | Final clock |
  Outcome |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Hang 3000 then ok | 2 | 3000, then 600 (4000 − 3400, after the 400 ms pause)
  | 400 | 3400 | success |
  | Hang 2950 then ok | 2 | 3000, 650 | 400 | 3350 | success |
  | Hang 2600 then ok | 2 | 3000, 1000 | 400 | 3000 | success |
  | 500 then ok | 2 | 3000, 3000 | 400 | 400 | success |
  | 500 then 500 | 2 | 3000, 3000 | 400 | 400 | throws `telegram send failed:
  500 Bad Gateway` |
  | 429 `retry_after` 1 at t=0 | 2 | 3000, 3000 | 1000 | 1000 | success |
  | 429 `retry_after` 1 after a 3 s hang | 1 | 3000 | none | 3000 | throws
  `telegram send failed: 429` |

    - The retry guard is `remaining < 1000 || remaining <= pause` (l.228). The 3
  s then 429 case is skipped because remaining is 1000 and the pause is 1000.
    - Still 2 attempts max, 400 ms pause, no retry on other 4xx (`retry = status
  >= 500`).
  - **(b) Description prefix** — ADDRESSED. `split(':')[0].trim().slice(0,60)`
  (l.137-141). Input `'Bad Request: chat not found "+375291234567"'` gives
  exactly `telegram send failed: 400 Bad Request` (test l.644-653). A 500-char
  `x` description is capped at 60 chars, and the test asserts the exact string.
  - **(c) Tests** — ADDRESSED.
    - Real `DOMException` `TimeoutError` is counted as an attempt and retried,
  with timeouts asserted as `[3000, 600]`.
    - Both-attempts-hang test asserts exactly 2 calls, clock ≤ 4000 and the
  exact message `telegram send failed: TimeoutError`.
    - No-retry-under-1000-ms test: a 3200 ms hang gives 1 call.
    - 429-skipped test: a 3 s delay then 429 gives 1 call.
    - Network `TypeError` followed by success is retried (2 calls).
    - `assertSafe` now also inspects `inspect(e, {showHidden:true})`.
    - Non-vacuity test `assertSafe(new Error(TOKEN))` throws.
  - **(d) `formatMinskTime` on invalid input** — ADDRESSED (l.31-39).
  `'garbage'` returns the raw input, with a test.
  - **(e) Body consumed on success** — ADDRESSED. l.213 is `await
  res.body?.cancel().catch(() => {})`. A stub `{ok:true,status:200}` with no
  `body` returned without throwing (1 call, no error).
  - **Surrogate pairs and trailing `…`** — ADDRESSED. `clip` uses `Array.from`
  (code points). The emoji cases above have no lone surrogates, and truncated
  values end in `…`.

  ### New Breakage in the Fix Diff
  - Minor, lib/telegram.ts:226-229: the guard only requires `remaining > pause`
  (strictly, and ≥ 1000). A 429 `retry_after` 1 arriving with 1001 ms remaining
  would sleep 1000 ms and then run the second attempt with a 1 ms timeout
  (`Math.max(1, …)`). That wastes the attempt but stays within the budget, and
  it is an unlikely edge. Requiring `remaining - pause >= ~500` would be
  cleaner.
  - Minor, lib/telegram.ts:1-232 and tests/telegram.test.ts:1-439: the whole of
  both files was reformatted from single quotes to double quotes, which inflates
  the diff (+419/−158). The repo has no prettier config, only eslint, so this
  is churn rather than a defect.
  - Files changed are only lib/telegram.ts and tests/telegram.test.ts, as
  expected. There is no hardcoded token or URL: `TOKEN` in tests is the fake
  `123456:SECRET-token_abc`, and the real URL is built from `process.env`.

  ### Secrets check (C)
  - The thrown error is `new Error("telegram send failed: " + last)`, with no
  `cause`. In my runs `e.cause` was `undefined`.
  - `last` is only `<status>`, `<status> <pre-colon description, ≤60>`, or
  `error.name`.
  - The only console output is the fixed `warn` string, once. The test spies on
  log, warn, error, info and debug.

  ### Out-of-Scope Observations
  - None.

  ### Verdict
  **Fix round:** All findings addressed, no new Critical/Important breakage.
  Remaining items are Minor: the comment is dropped when attribution is
  adversarially escape-heavy, the 1 ms timeout edge after a long 429 wait, and
  the formatting churn.
