› Message from general-purpose
  ### Spec Compliance
  - ❌ Issues found:
    1. **The double-click test (required test 1) doesn't test the in-flight ref
  (proved by mutation).** I copied `LeadForm.tsx` to the scratchpad, deleted
  only the line `if (inFlight.current) return;`, and ran the original
  `tests/lead-form.test.tsx` against that copy. All 8 tests passed, the
  double-click test included.
       - Why: each `fireEvent.click` runs inside `act`, so `status='sending'` is
  committed and the button is `disabled` before the second click. jsdom then
  skips the disabled button's activation. The test only checks `disabled`, which
  the brief says is not enough on its own.
       - A test that would catch it: `fireEvent.submit(form)` twice in the same
  tick (the Enter-key path). The ref-less copy fails it ("called 2 times"); the
  real component passes.
    2. **An idempotency key that isn't tied to the payload silently drops
  edits** (risk 2; details under Important).
    - Everything else in the brief is present. The copy is exact (fields, hints,
  consent, sending/failure/success copy, OrderSection eyebrow, heading and
  accent, paragraph, checklist, 3 steps). Endpoint and payload field names are
  right; the parity table calls the real `parseLead`.
    - Deviation, acceptable: native radios and `OutlinedInput` are used instead
  of MUI `TextField`/`Radio`.
  - ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff:
    - Whether React 19 keeps values typed before hydration in controlled inputs.
  This matters for the fast-fill and pre-hydration-submit paths below.
    - Real-browser rendering of the validation-error and success states (the
  implementer didn't screenshot them).
    - First Load JS / LeadForm chunk size.
    - The Pixso desktop/mobile slices 4 and 5 were not compared pixel by pixel.
  I only checked form.png: it shows an error icon inside invalid inputs, and the
  implementation shows it only under the field.

  ### Strengths
  - The submit guard itself is correct. `onSubmit` calls `preventDefault`, then
  checks `inFlight.current` first. The ref is set synchronously after client
  validation, before any `await` or state update, and reset in `finally`.
  Because it sits in the form submit handler, Enter-key submits are covered too
  (my `fireEvent.submit` ×2 experiment sends exactly 1 fetch).
  - Key lifecycle mostly matches the brief:
    - Created lazily at the first submit (`readStoredKey() ?? generateKey()`)
  and stored in `kvarta_idem` before the send. Storage access is wrapped in
  try/catch, and stored keys are validated with `/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{8,64}$/`.
    - Kept on 400/failed/timeout. Cleared on `ok` (including `duplicate`) and in
  `reset()`.
    - Traces: 503 → retry → same key (asserted by test 4). Reload mid-failure →
  `keyRef` is null, so the stored key is read back. Success → "another one" →
  `keyRef=null`, storage removed, and the next submit generates a new key. I
  confirmed `sessionStorage` is null after success.
  - Payload is exact:
    - `website` comes from the honeypot ref and is `''` in practice.
  `renderedAt` is a number.
    - `telegram` is trimmed with `@` stripped; `phone` is normalized to E.164;
  name and comment are trimmed.
    - `attribution` is `safeAttribution()` (try/catch) spread, plus
  `submittedAt: String(Date.now())`.
  - `submitLead` handles every case: 15 s AbortController timeout cleared in
  `finally`, non-JSON body → failed, 200 with `duplicate` → ok, 400 with
  `errors` → invalid, anything else → server `message` or the generic text.
  - `plural` handles 11–14 correctly (tested 1/2/4/5/11/12/21/22/25).
  - `renderedAt` is a mount-only ref and is not refreshed on reset. That is
  right: a quick second lead after reset still clears the 3 s check because
  mount time is old.
  - Sending state keeps values (text inputs `readOnly`, radios `disabled`),
  shows spinner + "Sending…" and the "Don't close the page" line, and
  announces through `aria-live`.
  - Failure state: retry is the same submit button with the same key; focus
  moves to it. A `tel:` button and the Telegram link from `CONTACT` are shown.
  - Success copy is honest (ruling 4): masked phone, the nick line only when a
  nick was given, static hours, no bot button. Focus moves to the heading
  (`tabIndex=-1`).
  - Bundle: no zod / `lead-schema` / `store` / `handle-lead` in client code (the
  client imports only `lib/phone`, `lib/attribution`, `lib/contact` and
  tokens). MUI is imported by path. `OrderSection` has no `'use client'` and
  imports from `tokens`.
  - Contrast (hand-computed):

    | Pair | Ratio |
    |---|---|
    | error on card | 6.07 |
    | error on paper2 (summary box) | 4.95 |
    | inkSoft on card | 7.37 |
    | paperDim on ink | 9.14 |
    | paper on ink | 14.43 |
    | paper on ink2 | 11.22 |
    | yolk on ink | 9.16 |
    | field border on card | 4.31 (passes 3:1 for non-text) |

  - Honeypot: off-screen at `left:-10000px` (not `display:none`), `aria-hidden`
  wrapper, `tabIndex -1`, `autoComplete off`.
  - Labels use `htmlFor`; the interest group has `fieldset` + `legend`.
  `aria-invalid` and `aria-describedby` point at the matching `lead-<key>-error`
  / `-hint` ids.
  - Phone mask: typing '80291234567' ends as `+375 29 123-45-67` (tested).
  Backspacing from the end walks cleanly down to `''`. Backspace on a middle
  literal is a no-op and the caret jumps on mid-edits; that's normal for masks
  and doesn't trap the user. Paste works.

  ### Issues
  #### Critical (Must Fix)
  None.

  #### Important (Should Fix)
  1. **Editing fields after a failed send reuses the same key, so the edit is
  lost silently** (`components/LeadForm.tsx` `onSubmit`/`keyRef`; risk 2).
  Proved in the scratchpad: 503 → user changes the phone → "Retry sending"
  sends the same `idempotencyKey` with phone `+375291234567` first and
  `+375331112233` second.
     - Where it bites: after a 15 s client timeout where the server did save,
  `saveLead`'s `SET lead:<key> NX` does nothing and `claimNotification(key)`
  returns false. The server answers 200 `{ok, leadId}`, the UI shows "Request received", and the manager has only the old, possibly mistyped phone. Corrected
  name, interest or comment are lost the same way.
     - Reload makes it worse: the key survives in `sessionStorage` but the
  values do not. A user who re-types a different lead after reload reuses a key
  that may already be stored.
     - Fix: keep `{key, fingerprint}` in `kvarta_idem`, where the fingerprint is
  the normalized name / phone / telegram / interest / comment. Regenerate the
  key when the current payload's fingerprint differs from the one the key was
  last sent with. Add a test: 503 → edit phone → retry → key differs. Same
  payload → same key, as test 4 already checks.
  2. **Test 1 is vacuous for the ref guard** (see Spec item 1). Add or replace
  it with `fireEvent.submit(form); fireEvent.submit(form)` → fetch called once.
  That version fails when the ref check is removed.
  3. **Interest pre-select listeners override the user's choice.** `sync` runs
  on every `hashchange`/`popstate` and resets `interest` to the URL value.
     - Real path: land on `/?interest=order#order` (from the Range/Hero CTAs),
  pick "Question", click any in-page anchor (header `#order`, a nav item) or press
  Back. `interest` silently returns to `order`, and the submitted lead is
  mis-categorised.
     - The listeners add almost nothing: the CTAs are plain `<a
  href="/?interest=…#order">`, so changing the query is a full navigation that
  remounts the form anyway.
     - Fix: drop the two listeners, or apply the URL value only while the user
  hasn't touched the radios (a `userPicked` ref). No unmounted-update risk:
  cleanup removes both listeners.
  4. **Submitting before hydration (or with JS broken) does a native GET.** The
  `<form>` has no `method`/`action`, so a submit before React hydrates navigates
  to `/?name=…&phone=…&telegram=…&comment=…&website=`.
     - Effect: the lead is lost without any message, and the phone number lands
  in the URL, CDN/access logs, history and analytics.
     - It is unlikely on a fast page, but it's the conversion path, and any
  chunk-load failure makes it permanent.
     - Minimal fix: render the submit button `disabled` until a mount effect
  flips a `hydrated` flag, and add `method="post"` so personal data never goes
  into a query string.

  #### Minor (Nice to Have)
  - **Fast-fill false positive (risk 4).** `renderedAt` is taken in a mount
  effect, i.e. after hydration, not when the user first saw the form. Two paths
  can make a real lead look fast and get the server's fake success:
    - Values typed before hydration (if React keeps them) followed by a submit
  within 3 s of hydration.
    - A returning visitor landing on `#order`, using browser autofill and
  submitting within about 3 s.

    Smallest safe handling: `renderedAt = Math.round(performance.timeOrigin)`,
  falling back to the mount time. That is never later than anything the user
  did. The brief mandated mount time, so this is a controller-level decision;
  the reset flow is already correct.
  - **Unknown-only server 400 shows the wrong message.** A 400 whose errors are
  only unknown keys (`body`, `idempotencyKey`, `renderedAt`, `website`) shows
  "Check your internet and press “Retry”" and retries the same payload
  forever. When known and unknown keys are mixed, the unknown ones are dropped.
  It doesn't crash, and with valid keys it's practically unreachable. A specific
  message plus key regeneration for `idempotencyKey` errors would be better.
  `retryable` is always `true` and never read.
  - **"Call us" appears twice for 429/503** (cosmetic): the server's message
  already ends "or call us." and the form adds "Or call us: …".
  - **Focus after a server 400 on `interest`** targets a radio that is still
  `disabled` (status is `sending` at that moment), so focus may not land.
  - **Tests:**
    - Test 2 checks only that `aria-describedby` is truthy, not that it points
  at `lead-phone-error`, and uses `getAllByText(...).length > 0`.
    - Untested: focus moving to the first invalid field, the summary plural with
  2+ fields, reset ("another one" → new key, name refocused), key persistence
  across a remount/reload, the 400 path in the component, and the
  hashchange/popstate listeners.
    - `plural` isn't tested for 0, 14 or 111 (the code handles them).
  - **Focus rings:**
    - The chips' `:focus-within` ring also shows on mouse click;
  `:has(:focus-visible)` would limit it to keyboard.
    - The input focus "ring" is a yolk 3px shadow at about 1.8:1 against the
  card. The 2px ink border carries the real contrast. Acceptable, but not the
  3px ink/yolk outline the convention describes.
  - **No in-field trailing error icon** as on the board (the icon is only under
  the field).
  - **Success-state live region** is mounted already containing its text, so
  screen readers may not announce it; the heading focus covers it.
  - **Honeypot named `website`** (per the brief) can be filled by identity
  autofill in some password managers, which would give a silent fake success.
  The DOM `name` could be something neutral while the payload key stays
  `website`.

  ### Assessment
  **Task quality:** Needs fixes
  **Reasoning:** The submit guard, payload, response mapping and copy are
  correct. But a retry after editing a field reuses the idempotency key and can
  silently drop the corrected phone behind a success screen. The interest
  listeners can overwrite the user's choice, and the required double-click test
  passes even with the ref guard removed (proved by mutation). Experiment files
  are in `/tmp/claude-1000/-home-usr-projects-coffee/8c81cb6b-623b-4837-9e9b-0e0
  d383d80b2/scratchpad/t9mut/`.

  2. The double-click test is vacuous. The reviewer proved this by deleting the

  3. The URL-interest listeners can override a user's chip choice.

✻ Worked for 48s · done 21:16

❯ Tell me what I need to do in Telegram to create a bot and be able to send     
  messages                                                                      

● Our code needs two values: TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN and TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID. Here's how
  to get both.

  1. Create the bot
  1. In Telegram, open @BotFather (the official one, with the blue check).
  2. Send /newbot.
  3. Give it a display name, for example Kvarta leads.
  4. Give it a username ending in bot, for example kvarta_leads_bot.
  5. BotFather replies with a token that looks like 123456789:AAH.... That's
     TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN. It's a password, so don't paste it into chat, a commit
     or a file in the repo. If it leaks, send /revoke to BotFather to get a new
     one.

  2. Choose where leads should arrive

  A bot can only message a chat that has talked to it first.

  - Option A, a private chat with the manager (simplest): the manager opens the
    bot and presses Start. They then send any message, for example "hi".
  - Option B, a group chat (better if several people handle leads): create a
    group, add the bot as a member, and send any message in the group. If the
    bot doesn't see messages, open BotFather, choose /mybots, then your bot,
    then Bot Settings, then Group Privacy, and turn it off.

  3. Get the chat id

  After you've sent that message, open this in a browser, with your real token
  in place of <TOKEN>:

  https://api.telegram.org/bot<TOKEN>/getUpdates

  Find "chat":{"id": ...} in the response. That number is TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID.
  - A private chat gives a positive number like 123456789.
  - A group gives a negative number like -1001234567890.

  If the response is empty ("result":[]), send another message to the bot or
  group and reload.

  4. Test it before deploying

  curl -s "https://api.telegram.org/bot<TOKEN>/sendMessage" \
    -d chat_id=<CHAT_ID> -d text="test from Kvarta"

  If the message arrives in the chat, the pair works.

  5. Put the values in Vercel, not in the code

  In the Vercel project go to Settings → Environment Variables and add
  TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN and TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID for Production. For local testing, put
  them in a .env.local file, which is git-ignored.

  Two things to know:
  - The bot only writes to the chat you chose. It can't message customers,
    because Telegram doesn't let a bot start a conversation with someone who
    hasn't pressed Start. That's why I changed the form so the customer's
    Telegram nick is optional, and the page never promises the customer a bot
    message.
  - Until the two variables are set, the site works normally and just skips the
    Telegram step. Leads are still saved in Redis.

  The page build is still in progress. Whenever you have the token and chat id,
  you can set them in Vercel yourself, or tell me and I'll walk you through it
  at deploy time.

  ### Finding Verdicts

  - **1. An edit after a failed send reused the same key, so the edit was
  silently lost**: **ADDRESSED.**
    - **Fingerprint.** `leadFingerprint` (lib/lead-client.ts:466-469) is
  `JSON.stringify([name, phone, telegram, interest, comment])`. It is taken from
  the `buildPayload` output (lib/lead-client.ts ~70-78), so the fields are
  already normalized: name and comment trimmed, phone in E.164 form, nick
  trimmed with the leading '@' removed, interest as the enum value. Key,
  honeypot, renderedAt and attribution are left out.
    - **Key logic.** components/LeadForm.tsx onSubmit (diff 225-236): build a
  draft, take its fingerprint, use `keyRef.current ?? readStoredKey()`, and
  generate a new key when there is no record or `record.fp !== fp`. The record
  is then written to memory and to `kvarta_idem` as JSON `{key, fp}`.
    - **Traces.** (i) and (ii) I ran in a scratch test; (iii) and (vi) I ran
  too.
      - (i) submit, 503, retry with no edit: same key (existing test "retries
  with the same key", diff 839-864).
      - (ii) submit, 503, edit phone, retry: new key, and the stored record is
  replaced (scratch check: stored key equals the third request's key). Existing
  test at diff 868-888.
      - (iii) edit and then revert before retrying: same key, because the
  fingerprint matches (scratch test passed). If the user sent the edited version
  (503) before reverting, the reverted send gets a third key. That is harmless:
  the server's phone claim catches it.
      - (iv) remount mid-failure with the same values: same key. With different
  values: new key (test at diff 892-921). sessionStorage survives a reload, so a
  reload behaves the same.
      - (v) success: `keyRef` and storage are cleared, and reset clears them
  again. The next send gets a new key (test at diff 952-969).
      - (vi) old-format storage values (a bare UUID, `12345678`, `"abcdefgh12"`,
  `{bad`): `JSON.parse` either throws and is caught, or returns a non-object,
  which is treated as absent. A valid new key is generated each time (scratch
  test passed). No crash.
    - **Tradeoff (server trace, lib/handle-lead.ts).**
      - Same phone, edited name, first request already saved: `claimPhone(P,
  k2)` returns k1, and k1 is not k2, so the server answers 200 `{duplicate:true,
  leadId:k1}`. The UI shows success, and the manager has only the first lead
  with the old name.
      - Edited phone: the new phone is claimed, so a second lead is saved and
  the manager gets both. That is the intended outcome.
      - **Minor:** the same-phone, edited-name case is inherently ambiguous
  because the server cannot tell a correction from a duplicate. Nothing is worse
  than before. I recommend calling it out in docs, not fixing it.

  - **2. Test 1 was vacuous for the ref guard**: **ADDRESSED.**
    - New test at diff 660-671 submits the form twice with
  `fireEvent.submit(form)`.
    - **Mutation M1** (deleted `if (inFlight.current) return;`,
  LeadForm.tsx:268): the test **fails** with "expected vi.fn() to be called 1
  times, but got 2 times".
    - **Mutation M2** (`disabled={pending || !hydrated}` changed to
  `disabled={!hydrated}`, ref guard kept): both submit-guard tests **pass**. So
  the guard is tested independently of the disabled state.

  - **3. The `?interest=` pre-select listeners overrode the user's choice**:
  **ADDRESSED.**
    - The mount effect (diff 147-166) reads `?interest=` once. No `hashchange`
  or `popstate` listener remains.
    - New test (diff 1053-1062): pick "Question", fire both events, and the choice
  stays.
    - **Mutation M3** (old `sync` listeners put back): the test **fails**, so it
  is not vacuous.

  - **4. Submitting before hydration did a native GET**: **ADDRESSED.**
    - The form now has `method="post"` (diff 311), and the button is
  `disabled={pending || !hydrated}` (LeadForm.tsx:720).
    - A test (diff 691-698) checks with `renderToString` that the markup has
  `method="post"` and a disabled submit button, and that the button is enabled
  after mount.
    - **What the user sees before hydration:**
      - The label "Send request" is shown, not a spinner. That spinner only
  appears while `pending`.
      - The disabled style is the yolk colour at 0.85 opacity.
      - Because the form's only submit button is disabled, pressing Enter does
  not submit the form natively.
    - **If the mount effect never runs (JS error):** the button stays disabled
  with no notice inside the form. The Footer (components/Footer.tsx:66,71)
  always shows the phone and Telegram links, so a fallback contact exists on the
  page. **Minor:** there is no in-form `<noscript>` hint.
    - `event.preventDefault()` is the first statement in onSubmit
  (LeadForm.tsx:267).

  - **E. renderedAt from performance.timeOrigin**: **OK.**
    - `pageStart()` (diff 97-100) returns `Math.round(timeOrigin)`, or
  `Date.now()` at mount when timeOrigin is not finite or not above 0. It is not
  refreshed on reset.
    - timeOrigin is navigation start, earlier than the old mount time. So
  `submittedAt - renderedAt` is at least the page age, and a real user is less
  likely than before to hit the 3000 ms rule.
    - **Skew.**
      - Rounding (Math.round, Safari, Firefox reduced timer precision) is at
  most about 100 ms, and only Firefox resistFingerprinting reaches that.
      - The only real risk is the wall clock moving backwards (an NTP step)
  during the visit, and it bites only when the backward step is bigger than the
  page age minus 3 s. That is negligible.
      - The bot check is slightly weaker because load time now counts toward the
  3 s. Acceptable.
    - **Fallback test:** it stubs timeOrigin as 1_700_000_000_000.4 (expects
  1_700_000_000_000) and as NaN (expects a value within the mount window).
  Mutation M8 (`Date.now()` only) makes it fail.

  - **F. Minors**: **all present and non-vacuous.**
    - (b) The honeypot id/name is `hp_field_x7`, with tabIndex -1, autoComplete

  'spam'`.
    - (c) A 400 with only unknown keys shows the failure box "Could not send the request" plus "Refresh the page and try again. Your form data is saved.", clears the key and keeps the values. Mutation M5 (key not
  cleared) fails the test.
      - A mixed 400 shows the known field errors and puts the unknown messages
  in the summary (tested).
      - `retryable` has no remaining code consumers; only two test titles still
  contain the word.
    - (d) The call-us hint dedupe uses the regex `/call us\.?\s*$/i`. It
  matches the server's real `UNAVAILABLE` message, "…or call us." (I

    - (e) Focus after a server 400 is deferred through `pendingFocus` and
  applied once status is back to idle. Mutation M6 (immediate focus) fails with
  "expected 'submit' to be 'radio'".
    - (f) Each requested test exists and asserts concrete values:
      - the `aria-describedby` target is `lead-phone-error`, holding the exact
  message

      - reset clears storage, refocuses name and produces a new key
      - remount persistence holds
      - the component 400 paths

  ### New Breakage in the Fix Diff
  - **Minor**, components/LeadForm.tsx:335 with `setField` at 239-251: after a
  mixed 400, `extraMessages` is never cleared when the user fixes the field.
    - Reproduced in scratch: 400 `{phone, website}`, then fix the phone. The
  summary then reads "Fix 0 fields / Check: . Errors are marked under the fields. / Invalid value".
    - It only happens when the client and server disagree, but the text is
  broken.
    - Fix: clear `extraMessages` when `errorKeys` becomes empty, or render the
  count and "Check" lines only when `errorKeys.length > 0`.
  - **Minor**: `extraMessages.map` uses `key={message}`. Two unknown keys with
  the same message would collide (React warning only).
  - **Minor (privacy)**: `kvarta_idem` now keeps the fingerprint, which includes
  the name, E.164 phone, nick and comment, in sessionStorage. Before, only an
  opaque key was stored. This is tab-scoped and cleared on success or reset, but
  storing a hash of the fingerprint would avoid holding personal data.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● The bot will send the customer one message. Before that, one Telegram rule
  shapes how it works.

  Why a username isn't enough: a Telegram bot can't message someone by their
  @name. It can only write to a numeric chat id, and it receives that id only
  after the person has opened the bot and pressed Start once. There's no way
  around this, which is why your Pixso design has the "Press Start in the bot"
  step.

  What I'm building (Task 13, running now):
  1. After the customer submits the form, the success screen shows an "Open the bot in Telegram" button. It links to t.me/<your_bot>?start=<lead id>.
  2. The customer taps it and presses Start. Telegram then sends our site a
     small update saying "this person started the bot for lead X".
  3. The site replies with one message, for example: "Hi, Anna! Request received: subscription, 2 bags of 250 g. The manager will call… Next roast:
     Thursday, 22 October."
  4. After that the bot stays silent. It ignores everything else, never reads or
     answers the customer's messages, and stores no chat id. A second Start
     doesn't send a second message.

  The customer's Telegram name field stays optional. The manager sees it in the
  lead notification, but the confirmation doesn't depend on it.

  What you'll need to do at deploy time (I'll write it up in
  docs/telegram-bot.md):
  - Create the bot with @BotFather, as I described earlier.
  - Add three environment variables in Vercel: TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN,
    NEXT_PUBLIC_TELEGRAM_BOT_USERNAME and TELEGRAM_WEBHOOK_SECRET. The secret is
    a random string, which openssl rand -hex 24 generates.
  - Run one setWebhook command after the first deploy, pointing Telegram at
    https://<your-domain>/api/telegram/webhook.

  I can't test the real Telegram round trip myself, because that needs your bot
  token and a deployed URL. I'll test everything up to that point locally.

  Task 10 (page assembly, SEO and the link-preview image) is running in parallel
  with Task 13. I'm waiting on both.

  for the guard.

  ### Strengths

  tabIndex={-1}>` with Hero, Week, Range, Subscription, Delivery, About, Faq,

  ### Issues
  #### Critical (Must Fix)
  None.

  #### Important (Should Fix)
  1. **Unguarded metadataBase (lib/site.ts).**
  `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL=kvarta.vercel.app` or `=""` throws `Invalid URL` in `new
  URL(SITE_URL)` and fails the production build. The robots and sitemap URLs
  would also be malformed without a scheme. Smallest fix: normalise in
  lib/site.ts. Trim the value, prefix `https://` when it does not match
  `/^https?:\/\//`, treat an empty string as unset (use `||`, not `??`), and
  optionally wrap `new URL` in a try/catch that falls back to localhost.
  2. **Dead skip link on /privacy.** The privacy `<main>` has no `id="main"` or
  `tabIndex={-1}`. Fix: add `id="main" tabIndex={-1}` to that main. This is a
  small change in a file where only the title was allowed to change, so
  coordinate it with Task 13's ownership of the page.
  3. **Hardcoded hex in app/globals.css (3 values, skip link only).** `#f3ebdd`
  equals `colors.paper`, and `#231a14` (twice) equals `colors.ink` (compared
  with theme/tokens.ts, case-insensitive). The values match, and no other hex is
  hardcoded in the diff. The repo rule is "tokens only", and these values will
  drift silently if the tokens change. I rate it Important to follow the stated
  rule. If the controller wants a gentler call, Minor is defensible because the
  values are equal and limited to the skip link. Smallest fix: replace
  `.skip-link` with a tiny server component, `components/SkipLink.tsx`, that
  renders `Box component="a" href="#main"` with `sx` using `colors.paper` and
  `colors.ink`. Use `transform: 'translateY(-200%)'` and `'&:focus-visible': {
  transform: 'none', outline: \`3px solid ${colors.ink}\`, outlineOffset: '2px'
  }`. Keep only the `scroll-behavior` media query in globals.css.

  #### Minor (Nice to Have)
  - **app/icon.svg** is valid: it has a viewBox, no external references, and
  uses the paper and roast hex values. It is a standalone asset, so hardcoded
  colors are unavoidable there. The quarter-circle is large and legible at 16px.
  - **`<main style={{ outline: 'none' }}>`** is fine, since main is only
  programmatically focusable. Moving it to sx or CSS would be tidier.
  - **Dependency placement.** `@fontsource/roboto` is in `dependencies`, which
  is safe even if the route becomes dynamic later. The lockfile diff was
  excluded, so I only confirmed package.json.
  - **Hardcoded texts.** `'roastery · Minsk · since 2019'` and the headline are
  duplicated between lib/og-image.tsx and the components. Acceptable.
  - **Logo label mismatch (named risk 11, fix round).** The diagnosis is
  confirmed. components/Logo.tsx has `aria-label="Kvarta, to the home page"`, while
  the visible text is "Kvarta" plus the caption "roastery · Minsk", so the
  accessible name does not start with the visible text (WCAG 2.5.3, Lighthouse
  label-content-name-mismatch). The one-line fix is `aria-label="Kvarta roastery · Minsk, to the home page"`. The same Logo component is used in Header, in Footer
  with `tone="onInk"`, and in app/privacy/page.tsx, so one change covers all
  three.

● Agent(Security review of Task 13 bot webhook) Opus 5.5
  ⎿  Backgrounded agent (↓ to manage · ctrl+o to expand)

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Here's how the customer reaches the bot without searching. The first piece is
  built and the others follow in the next task.

  1. The button (built). "Open the bot in Telegram" opens the bot chat directly.
  Telegram shows a big Start button, and one tap does everything. No search is
  involved.

  2. A QR code for desktop visitors (next task). The same link also appears as a
  QR code, so someone on a laptop can scan it with their phone.

  3. Finding the bot by hand, no link (next task). This is the case you
  described: the customer finds the bot themselves and just types "start". It
  works through the Telegram name they entered in the form. Telegram attaches
  the sender's verified username to every message and it can't be faked. So:
  - When the lead is saved with a Telegram name, the site remembers "this
    username goes with this lead" for 24 hours.
  - If a customer writes /start, "start", "[Russian: start]" or "[Russian: hello]" to the bot, the
    bot checks their username, finds the lead and sends the same single
    confirmation.
  - The box on the success screen shows the bot's name, so they can type it into
    Telegram's search.

  Limits you should know about:
  - A customer with no Telegram username can only use the button or the QR code.
  - The bot stays one-message-only. It ignores anything else and never replies
    to unmatched text.
  - If someone types another person's username in the form, that person would
    get a confirmation about the stranger's lead when they press Start. That's a
    small, accepted risk, limited by the 24-hour expiry and the one-time rule.
    I'll document it.

  One security problem I'm checking (the reviewer is on it now). The first
  version builds the bot link from the server's lead id, even when the server
  says "duplicate". In that case the id belongs to a different customer who
  submitted the same phone number minutes earlier. So an attacker could resubmit
  a victim's phone number and then open the bot with the victim's id. The bot
  would send the attacker the victim's name and phone, and use up the victim's
  one-time confirmation. The fix will be to build the link only from the
  customer's own request id, and to hide the bot box on duplicates. I'll apply
  that along with whatever else the review finds.

  Task 10's fix round is still running too.

✻ Worked for 1m 46s · done 21:30

› Message from general-purpose
  ### Spec Compliance
  - ❌ Issues found:
    1. **The duplicate-lead link leaks another customer's data (deviation 2).**
  The brief says the start payload is "the idempotency key the request used".
  The implementation uses the server's `result.leadId` instead, and a test locks
  that in (`tests/lead-form-bot.test.tsx`, "uses the stored lead id from a
  duplicate answer"). This opens a privacy hole (see Critical).
    2. The brief places the yolk button inside the box. Here it is a sibling
  after the box (`components/LeadForm.tsx` success view). This is cosmetic.
  - ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff:
    - The real Upstash `get` auto-parse behaviour against live Redis. Only the
  mock is exercised, though both branches are coded.
    - The build table (route shown as dynamic) and the production-server 401/200
  checks. I am relying on the report for these.
    - How the box looks compared with form.png. No screenshot was taken.
    - That the full suite is green. I did not run it.

  ### Strengths
  - **Authentication:** `secretMatches` fails closed. An empty or unset
  `TELEGRAM_WEBHOOK_SECRET` makes `!secret` false, so an empty secret with a
  missing header gives 401. It compares sha256 digests with `timingSafeEqual`,
  so lengths always match and nothing throws. Auth runs before `request.text()`,
  and only `POST` is exported. There is no segment config, and `node:crypto`
  runs on the default Node runtime.
  - **Parsing:** `parseStart` is fully defensive. A missing `message`,
  non-string `text`, non-object `chat`, non-private chat, or a string, float or
  unsafe chat id all return null, and nothing can throw. The regex is anchored
  `^...$` with `\s+`, an 8-64 payload of `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`, and an optional
  `@bot`. Invalid JSON gives 200, and so does a store error. There is no path
  that echoes user text or replies to an error, and the only
  `sendTelegramMessage` call is on the happy path.
  - **One message per lead:** the marker is claimed before the send (`SET
  tgstart:<id> 1 NX EX 2592000` on the same client). It is released only if the
  send fails, and the response is 200 either way. The memory store's claim is a
  synchronous check-and-add, so the `Promise.all` test genuinely races at the
  microtask level.
  - **Logs and errors:** only `safeErrorLabel` strings are logged.
  `sendTelegramMessage` errors carry `error.name` or the status plus a capped
  description, never the URL or token. The 401/413 bodies are `{ok:false}`. The
  log test uses `inspect(..., {showHidden:true})` and has a non-vacuity probe
  (`toContain('webhook')`).
  - **Message content:** the name is escaped. The phone, interest and roast text
  are not escaped, but they come from fixed or validated sources. There is no
  comment, nick, email or attribution. `isOrderOpen` is honoured and `now` is
  injected. The "This is the only message…" line is present, and the
  worst-case length is about 400 chars.
  - **Store:** `parseStoredLead` handles both a JSON string and an
  already-parsed object. It rejects arrays, partial objects, a bad `interest`
  and a non-string attribution. The Upstash mock asserts the exact `{nx:true,
  ex:2_592_000}`.
  - **Refactor:** `tests/telegram.test.ts` changes are additive only (one import
  and a new describe block). `sendTelegram` keeps its missing-config
  warn-and-return behaviour. The `nextRoastText` extraction is clean and its
  tests are re-pointed.
  - **Contrast** (computed): ink-soft on paper-2 is 6.02, ink on paper-2 is
  12.95, and ink on yolk (the contained button) is 9.16.
  - **Docs and env:** `.env.example` has names only. The docs use `<TOKEN>` and
  `<SECRET>` placeholders. The only token-shaped string in the diff is the fake
  test fixture `123456:SECRET-token_abc`.

  ### Issues
  #### Critical (Must Fix)
  1. **A duplicate submission lets an attacker read another customer's name and
  phone and use up their greeting** (`components/LeadForm.tsx` around the
  `startId` line; `lib/handle-lead.ts` duplicate branch;
  `app/api/telegram/webhook/route.ts`). The code allows it today. Trace:
     1. The victim submits phone P and name N. `claimPhone` stores P with lead
  id V, and `saveLead` writes `lead:V`.
     2. Within 10 minutes the attacker submits P with any name and their own key
  K. `claimPhone` returns V, which is not K, so `handleLead` answers `200
  {ok:true, leadId:V, duplicate:true}`.
     3. `startId = IDEM_RE.test("V") ? "V" : K` gives V, and the link becomes
  `https://t.me/<bot>?start=V`.
     4. The attacker presses Start. Telegram posts `/start V` from the
  attacker's private chat, auth passes because it really is Telegram, and
  `getLead(V)` returns the victim's lead.
     5. `claimBotGreeting(V)` returns true and
  `sendTelegramMessage(attackerChat, …)` sends "Hi, N!… The manager will call
  +375 P…" plus the interest.
     6. The victim's own Start later finds the marker taken and gets nothing for
  30 days.

     The attacker already knows P, but learns N (often a full name despite the
  hint) and the interest, and silently takes the victim's single confirmation. A
  UI-only fix is not enough. The API already returns V in the JSON, so an
  attacker can build the t.me link by hand with `curl`.

     Smallest fix, both parts:
     - **(a) Client:** build the link only from `record.key`. Hide the bot box
  when `result.duplicate` is true or `result.leadId !== record.key`. Invert the
  test that currently locks in the duplicate link.
     - **(b) Server:** in `handleLead`'s duplicate branch, return `leadId:
  lead.idempotencyKey`, not `existing`, so the response never discloses another
  lead's id. This requires updating `tests/handle-lead.test.ts:56`, which
  asserts the duplicate id equals the first one. The client never needs V.
     - **Optional hardening:** drop the phone from the greeting, or show only
  its last 2 digits, so a leaked id discloses less.

     Separately, `handleLead` logs `idempotencyKey` and the manager notification
  shows it. That predates this task, but those ids are now bearer tokens for a
  PII message.

  #### Important (Should Fix)
  - None beyond the above.

  #### Minor (Nice to Have)
  1. **Body cap:** `await request.text()` buffers the whole body before the
  20,000-char check, so the cap does not limit memory. This only matters after
  auth, so the risk is low. Checking `content-length` first, or reading a capped
  stream, would make the cap real.
  2. **Retry window (at-least-once):** if `fetch` times out after Telegram has
  already accepted the message, `sendTelegramMessage` may retry internally, and
  on a throw the marker is released. The customer can then receive a second
  message. This is acceptable as at-least-once, but it is worth a comment in the
  code and in the docs, which currently promise "nothing is sent".
  3. **Marker key mismatch:** `claimBotGreeting(lead.leadId)` but
  `releaseBotGreeting(start.leadId)`. They are equal in practice, but `getLead`
  never checks that the stored `leadId` matches the requested one. Use
  `start.leadId` for both.
  4. **Link building:** the href is not built with `encodeURIComponent`. It is
  safe today because the value is regex-checked or a UUID, but it is fragile.
  5. **Bot username check:** the validator `^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_]{4,31}$` does
  not require the `bot` suffix. It does hide the box for invalid values.
  6. **Docs:** there is no secret-rotation section, which needs `setWebhook`
  with the new `secret_token` and an env change and redeploy, ordered to avoid a
  401 gap.
  7. **Missing test cases:**
     - No test sends `/start` with an `is_bot` sender or a forwarded message.
  These are harmless: bots cannot DM bots, and a forwarded `/start V` needs V
  anyway.
     - The logging test does not assert that the lead id is absent. The code
  never logs it.

  ### Assessment
  **Task quality:** Needs fixes
  **Reasoning:** Authentication, input handling, the one-send marker, log
  hygiene and message escaping are solid and well tested. However, the
  deliberate "use the server's leadId on duplicate" choice, together with the
  API returning the existing lead's id, lets anyone who submits a victim's phone
  within 10 minutes receive the victim's name and phone in their own Telegram
  and use up the victim's greeting. Both the client and `handleLead` need to
  change.

● The reviewer confirmed the privacy hole end to end: the server returns another
  customer's lead id on a duplicate submission, and the first version of the
  bot link would have turned that into a way to read their name and phone. It
  needs a fix on both sides, because hiding the box in the page alone isn't
  enough: anyone can build the link by hand from the API response. I'm sending
  the fix back, along with the smaller points.

  `??`), prefixes `https://` when there is no http(s) scheme, and checks the

  `http://localhost:3000`. `SITE_URL = resolveSiteUrl({...process.env.*})`.
  `app/layout.tsx:28` (`new URL(SITE_URL)`), `app/robots.ts:5` and

  1. Minor, `lib/site.ts:13-14`: a non-http scheme such as `'ftp://x.example'`

✻ Crunched for 34s · done 21:43

› Message from general-purpose
  ### Finding Verdicts
  - **CRITICAL: a duplicate submission leaked the victim's lead id, letting an
  attacker get the victim's greeting and use up their marker.** ADDRESSED.
    - **Server (b):** `lib/handle-lead.ts:67-69` now returns `{ok:true, leadId:
  lead.idempotencyKey, duplicate:true}`.
    - **Probe** (two keys, same phone, memory store). Victim gets
  `{"leadId":"VICTIM-KEY-0001"}`. Attacker gets `{"status":200,"body":{"ok":true
  ,"leadId":"ATTACK-KEY-0002","duplicate":true}}`. Neither
  `util.inspect(showHidden)` nor JSON of the attacker's response contains the
  victim's id or name.
    - **Other paths that could expose V:**
      - The 503 bodies are fixed strings.
      - The duplicate branch logs nothing.
      - The manager notification (`lib/telegram.ts:80`, "id V") goes only to the
  trusted manager chat.
      - The greeting is sent only to the chat that presents V.
      - V is a `crypto.randomUUID()` (`components/LeadForm.tsx:61-67`) kept in
  the victim's own localStorage.
      - Conclusion: the attacker has no read path to V.
    - **Client (a):** `components/LeadForm.tsx:206`: `botKey = !result.duplicate
  && result.leadId === record.key && IDEM_RE.test(record.key) ? record.key :
  null`. Both the box and the button need `BOT_USERNAME && done.leadId` (`:230`,
  `:244`), so the attacker's duplicate shows the success screen with no bot
  box.
    - **Branches of handleLead:**
      - New lead → own key, box shown.
      - Own-key replay: `claimPhone` returns the own key, so the code falls
  through. `saveLead` is SET NX (a no-op), the answer is the own key with no
  duplicate flag, and the box stays.
      - Foreign claim → own key plus `duplicate:true`, box hidden. The
  "conservative" client test is exactly this real server shape, so it is
  load-bearing.
      - Honeypot and fast-fill fake successes (`:37`) → own key, no flag, box
  shown. The `?start=K` link points to a lead that was never stored. The
  webhook's `getLead(K)` returns null, so it answers 200 and sends nothing.
  Harmless.
    - **Mutation G (scratch copy):**
      - Reverting (b) to `leadId: existing` fails `tests/handle-lead.test.ts >
  dedupes the same phone within 10 minutes` ("expected 'key-0001' to be
  'key-0002'").
      - Reverting (a) to the old `IDEM_RE.test(result.leadId) ? result.leadId :
  record.key` fails 3 of 5 tests in `tests/lead-form-bot.test.tsx` (foreign
  duplicate, duplicate with own id, non-duplicate with a foreign id).
  - **Minor 1: body cap.** ADDRESSED.
    - `route.ts:87-90` checks content-length first, then `readCapped`
  (`:39-55`). Auth (`:81`) still comes before both: a request with no secret and
  content-length 99999 got 401.
    - Probes:
      - content-length 20001 → 413, no send.
      - Garbage content-length (NaN) → the check is skipped and the body is read
  normally → 200.
      - No content-length, 25,000-char stream → 413. The reader is cancelled
  once it passes the cap, and the repo test shows fewer than 100 pulls on an
  endless stream.
      - Exactly 20,000 chars → 200 and the message is sent. 20,001 → 413.
      - Null body → 200. Empty string → 200 (JSON.parse fails, ok()).
      - Invalid UTF-8 → 200 (the TextDecoder is not fatal, so it substitutes
  U+FFFD).
    - The bytes-vs-characters mismatch is harmless for memory: the decoded
  string is at most ~20k chars. See the Minor under New Breakage for its retry
  side effect.
  - **Minor 2: at-least-once note.** ADDRESSED: `route.ts:111-112` and
  `docs/telegram-bot.md` ("Delivery is at-least-once, not exactly-once…"). Step
  4's "Press Start again: nothing is sent" describes the success case, and the
  note follows directly, which is acceptable.
  - **Minor 3: one lead id for claim and release.** ADDRESSED: `route.ts:108`
  (`lead.leadId !== start.leadId` → ok, no send), `:109` claims with
  `start.leadId`, `:119` releases with `start.leadId`. Tests cover the mismatch
  case and claim/release with the requested id.
  - **Minor 4: encodeURIComponent.** ADDRESSED (`LeadForm.tsx:250`).
  - **Minor 5: bot username validator.** ADDRESSED (`lib/bot-username.ts:5-8`).
    - Accepted: 'kvarta_bot', 'KvartaBot', 'my_bot', and '@kvarta_bot' →
  'kvarta_bot'.
    - Hidden: 'bot', 'kvartabot1', '', undefined, 'kvarta', 'kvarta_roastery',
  and the 33-char name.
    - The UI copy is unchanged.
  - **Minor 6: docs.** ADDRESSED.
    - The rotation section is correct and uses only placeholders (`<NEW
  SECRET>`, `openssl rand`); no real token or secret.
    - The privacy note names the typed name plus the masked phone and explains
  the own-key-only link.
    - `.env.example` has no diff.
  - **Minor 7: extra tests.** ADDRESSED: is_bot, `forward_origin` (the code also
  catches `forward_date`), and 'LEAD-SECRET-ID-1234' absent from all console
  output in 3 failure paths (inspected with showHidden).
  - **Greeting content (B).** `lib/customer-message.ts:33-38`, probed with all
  four operator codes: "+375 25/29/33/44 ***-**-67".
    - The name is HTML-escaped (`<a>&"` → `&lt;a&gt;&amp;&quot;`).
    - No comment, nick, UTM data or middle digits; the length is a fixed 278
  chars plus the name, which the schema caps at 60.
    - The exact full string is asserted in `tests/customer-message.test.ts`, the
  44 code by substring.

  ### New Breakage in the Fix Diff
  - **Minor: `app/api/telegram/webhook/route.ts:87-88`.** The new byte-based
  content-length check can return 413 to genuine, authenticated Telegram
  updates.
    - Example: a user replies to their own 4096-character CJK message with
  another one. Telegram embeds `reply_to_message`, so the update is about 25 KB
  in UTF-8 but only about 8–9k characters. The old character-based check let it
  through; the byte check rejects it.
    - Any non-2xx makes Telegram retry that update until it gives up, so pending
  updates pile up.
    - Suggested fix (outside this round's scope): once the secret is verified,
  answer 200 and drop oversized bodies instead of 413, or raise the cap to e.g.
  64 KB.
    - Not a security or privacy issue.
  - **Minor (test gap): `tests/handle-lead.test.ts:475-478`.** The duplicate
  test checks only `JSON.stringify(b.body)`, not `util.inspect` as the brief
  asked, and keeps the default name for both requests. My probe filled the gap
  (inspect with showHidden, different names: clean).
    - Changed assertions, both correct:
      - `b.leadId === a.leadId` became own key plus "never contains key-0001".
      - The lead-form "links to the bot" mock now echoes the request key instead
  of 'L1', which is required by the new rule.
      - The old duplicate-link test was inverted as instructed. The mask strings
  were updated to the masked form.
    - No test was weakened, and the concurrency paths are untouched.
  - Focused suite: 5 files, 92 tests pass in the scratch copy.

  ### Out-of-Scope Observations
  - Everything below predates the fix diff.
  - `lib/handle-lead.ts:64,85,89,99,105,109` log `idempotencyKey` / `leadId` in
  `console.error`. With the deep link, a lead id is a bearer token: whoever
  holds it can trigger the greeting (name plus masked phone). The risk is low
  because only operators can read the logs, but it conflicts with the "lead id
  never logged" rule the webhook now follows.
  - `components/LeadForm.tsx:67` falls back to a `Math.random`-based key when
  `crypto.randomUUID` is missing (non-secure context only). That key is now a
  bearer token, so it is weaker than it should be.
  - `route.ts:89`: a stream that errors in transport makes `reader.read()`
  throw, which gives a 500. The old `request.text()` behaved the same, and a
  Telegram retry is correct for a broken transfer.
  - `parseBotUsername` accepts names that start with a digit or underscore
  ('12345bot', '_____bot'), which Telegram itself rejects. This matches the
  specified rule and the value comes from the operator, so it is harmless.

  ### Verdict
  **Fix round:** All findings addressed, no new Critical/Important breakage (two
  Minors noted above).

› Message from general-purpose
  Mobile Performance on `/` is already 0.88 / 0.91 / 0.92 (median 0.91), so it
  clears 90 only barely. Nothing was changed or committed in the main checkout;
  the perf worktree and the port-3911 server are both removed.

  **Method:** Lighthouse 12.8.2 on the production build, using system Chrome
  (headless), mobile emulation, simulated throttling (RTT 150 ms, 1.6 Mbps, CPU
  x4), performance category only, 3 runs. Localhost has no real network latency,
  and run-to-run noise is about ±0.02.

  **Median metrics:**
  - FCP 1.10 s, LCP 3.01 s, TBT 237 ms, CLS 0, Speed Index 1.10 s, TTI 3.7 s.
  - Transfer 364 KiB, JS execution about 0.85 s.
  - LCP element is `h1#hero-title`. Its observed render delay is only 137 ms.
  Most of the simulated LCP comes from script CPU.

  **Experiments:**
  - Blocking all JS gave score 0.98, LCP 2.27 s, TBT 40 ms. So JS costs about 7
  score points.
  - Blocking `*.woff2` changed LCP by 0. Fonts are not the problem.

  **Top 5 weight contributors (gzip, JS actually loaded, about 196 KB):**
  1. react-dom, 72 KB.
  2. Next client runtime, about 58 KB.
  3. `@mui/material`, about 31 KB.
  4. `@mui/system` and `@mui/utils`, about 21 KB.
  5. Emotion, about 11 KB.

  Our own code is about 7 KB. The 39.5 KB `noModule` polyfill chunk is not
  downloaded by modern browsers.

  - zod, lead-schema, store, `@upstash/redis` and test libraries are not in any
  client chunk.
  - Every `@mui/material` component is a `'use client'` file. So the
  Box/Typography/Button/Link used in the server sections ship as client code and
  all of that tree hydrates.
  - HTML is 360 KB raw / 46 KB gzip, with a 206 KB `<head>`.
  - Emotion CSS in the head is duplicated on every page. The 3 style blocks
  appear twice (199 KB raw in total); the second copy costs about 9.5 KB gzip. I
  did not find the cause.
  - The inline RSC payload is 102 KB.

  **Ranked plan (details in the report):**
  1. Replace MUI Box/Typography/Button/Link in server sections with plain
  elements and static CSS, keeping MUI only for LeadForm. Saves about 25–60 KB
  gzip, 100 KB of inline CSS and 100 KB of RSC payload, worth +4–7 points. High
  effort, with design-fidelity risk.
  2. Fix the duplicated Emotion `<style>` blocks. Saves about 9.5 KB gzip and
  style-recalc time, +1–2 points. Cause unverified.
  3. Lazy-load LeadForm, only after step 1. Saves about 22 KB gzip. Skip it if
  `ssr:false` would remove the form from the HTML.
  4. Drop vendor prefixes with `stylisPlugins: []`. Saves 0.9 KB gzip, marginal.
  5. Skip the italic font change (visual change, 0 measured effect), the
  polyfill chunk, `optimizePackageImports` (already a default for
  `@mui/material`), `ssr:false` in server components (not allowed in Next 16)
  and Pigment CSS (not usable with Turbopack).

  **Feasibility:** ≥90 is plausible now but fails about 1 run in 3. Step 1 plus
  step 2 should give about 0.95–0.97, since the HTML-only ceiling measured 0.98.
  Biggest uncertainty: whether step 1 can stay pixel-identical, plus lab noise
  and real-device CPU.

  Full report with tables: /tmp/claude-1000/-home-usr-projects-coffee/8c81cb6b-6
  23b-4837-9e9b-0e0d383d80b2/scratchpad/perf-analysis.md

● Agent(Implement Task 15: Telegram channel instead of customer bot)
  ⎿  Backgrounded agent (↓ to manage · ctrl+o to expand)

● The new concept is simpler: when an order comes in, the bot posts it to a
  Telegram channel, and nothing is ever sent to the customer. Here's what the
  change covers.

  What changes in the app:
  - The "Telegram nick" field is removed from the form, including its
    validation, the server schema, the stored data and the consent and privacy
    wording.
  - The customer bot is removed: the "Open the bot" button, QR code, webhook
    route, confirmation message and their environment variables. The Task 13b
    work you stopped is dropped for good.
  - The existing order notification stays. It will post to a channel instead of
    a private chat, using the same TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID variable, which can now be a
    channel id.
  - The fix that stops the API leaking another customer's id on a duplicate
    stays.
  - The webhook route will return 404, so there's one less public endpoint to
    defend.

  Please set up the channel as private. Each post contains the customer's name
  and phone. A public channel would publish those numbers to anyone. I'm writing
  the docs to say so, and to make the bot an admin with only the "Post
  messages" permission.

  Setup you'll do, once the code is in (written up in docs/telegram-channel.md):
  1. Create the bot with @BotFather and note the token.
  2. Create a private channel and add the bot as an administrator who can post.
  3. Post one message in the channel, then open
     https://api.telegram.org/bot<TOKEN>/getUpdates. The channel id is the
     negative number starting with -100.
  4. In Vercel, set TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN and TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID.

  Two questions for you:
  1. Who should be in the channel: only the manager and staff? I assumed yes. If
     you want customers to see something there, that should be a separate
     public channel without personal data, and I'd build it as a separate
     feature.
  2. Should the site show a "subscribe to our channel" link? I haven't added
     one, since the channel is for orders.

  The full-width layout (with the contacts scroll fix) and this channel change
  are being built in parallel. After both are reviewed and merged, I'll finish
  the performance tuning, the README and the deploy.

  ### Issues
  #### Critical (Must Fix)
  None.

  #### Important (Should Fix)
  1. `components/Delivery.tsx:72`: `maxWidth: 560` applies at all widths and can
  change wrapping at 640-1023. Scope it, for example `maxWidth: { md: 560 }`
  (it is a no-op at 1440, where the card inner width is about 524). This keeps
  the below-1024 guarantee.

  #### Minor (Nice to Have)
  1. Hero headline widow at 1920/2560: "…on Thursday. At / your place on Friday" is
  visible in the screenshots. A CSS-only fix is `text-wrap: balance` on the hero
  Typography, or `pretty`. Do not add nbsp, which would change copy. Note the
  "At your place" break is already visible at 1920 in the final screenshot, not only at
  2560.
  2. Footer min-height also applies at 640-1023 (for example 768x1024, about 960
  px minimum). It can add an empty ink block in the footer on tablets. This is
  within the contacts-fix intent; at 390 the footer content already exceeds the
  minimum. Not a regression.
  3. Textarea `minHeight: 97` is a pixel value. It equals 3 rows of 23 px
  line-height plus 28 px padding, which is correct at default font size. With
  larger browser text, SSR would be shorter than the hydrated height and a small
  jump could return. An em-based value (`calc(3 * 1.4375em + 28px)`) or the
  `rows` approach would be more robust. It does not break at default settings.
  4. The Order `xl` gap `wide(80, ...)` starts at 80, but the current lg value
  is 80 and the xl value of 87.7 at 1536 gives about a 7 px step at the xl
  breakpoint (above 1024, negligible).
  5. The bag at 2560 is capped at 760 inside a 912 px column and left-aligned,
  leaving about 150 px beside it. Raising the cap or `justifySelf: end` would
  tidy it.
  6. The About and Order text columns are narrower than their grid cells at 2560
  (a deliberate readable measure, as the implementer noted).
  7. `100vw` in the clamps includes the scrollbar width, so values differ by
  8-15 px from the nominal ones when a scrollbar is present. This is harmless.

  **Task quality:** Needs fixes
  **Reasoning:** The CONTAINER_PX and typography math, the contacts-anchor
  structure and the wide-screen scaling check out, with no remaining cap that
  defeats the goal. One unscoped `maxWidth: 560` in Delivery can alter 640-1023
  rendering and must be limited to md; the rest is polish.

✻ Worked for 1m 47s · done 22:27

❯ The database will be file‑based on the server.                                

● A file database fits, with one caveat: it only works on a server with a
  persistent disk, that is, on your VPS, in Docker or under systemd. On Vercel
  the disk is temporary and requests would disappear after a restart. So I'm
  assuming the site will run on your server.

  I'll implement it like this:
  - One SQLite database in a file. It replaces Upstash, and Upstash stays as a
    fallback.
  - The same logic as in Redis. Duplicate protection by phone for 10 minutes,
    request rate limiting, one notification per request. Everything is atomic,
    and the database stays intact even if the site runs in several processes.
  - The file is protected. Access rights for the owner only, the data/ folder is
    in .gitignore, plus export and backup scripts.
  - A ready build for your server. Standalone mode, Dockerfile, docker-compose,
    an example nginx config, a systemd option and step-by-step instructions.

  I'll run this task in parallel with the others.

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

    server.

› Message from general-purpose
  ### Spec Compliance
  - ❌ Issues found: every requirement is implemented as specified, but one
  safeguard asked for in risk 1 is missing: there is no guard when
  `TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID` equals `TELEGRAM_CHANNEL_ID`. Details are under Important.
  - ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff:
    - The mutation check on the hostile-lead test (add the name to the channel
  message and watch it fail). I did not run it, because I can't build a scratch
  copy without touching the repo. By reading, it would fail: the lead's name is
  `<b>Anna</b>` and the forbidden list contains "Anna".
    - Live Telegram behavior and the real channel id configuration.
    - The rendered 44 px tap height in the footer (the code sets `minHeight: 44`
  via the existing `linkSx`).
  - The focused vitest run passed (52 of 52, `tests/telegram.test.ts` and
  `tests/footer.test.tsx`).

  **Risk traces**

  1. **Public feed privacy.**
     - `formatChannelMessage` interpolates only `INTEREST_LABELS[lead.interest]`
  and `formatMinskTime(lead.createdAt)`. No name, phone, comment, attribution
  or lead id is referenced.
     - For an unexpected `interest`, the map lookup returns `undefined`. The
  post would read "· undefined": the raw value is never printed, but there is no
  safe fallback label.
     - For an invalid `createdAt`, `formatMinskTime` returns the raw input
  string, unescaped. `createdAt` is set by the server, so this is low risk, but
  it is the one place a raw stored string reaches the public channel.
     - Wiring in `sendTelegram`: the private chat gets `formatLeadMessage(lead)`
  and the channel gets `formatChannelMessage(lead)`. There is no cross-wiring.
  The "targets" test checks both bodies by `chat_id` and by exact text.
     - **Equal-ids case.** If both variables hold the same id, the private
  target sends the full message to that id and the channel target sends the
  anonymized post. If that id is the public channel, customer phones are
  published. There is no guard.
  2. **Delivery rules.** Each case matches the code and a test:

     | Case | Result | Test |
     |---|---|---|
     | Both configured, both ok | Two sends | yes |
     | Private ok, channel fails | Resolves, one PII-free log line | yes |
     | Private fails, channel ok | Throws `telegram send failed: 400 Bad
  Request` | yes |
     | Both fail | Throws the private reason | no direct test (covered by the
  `priv` rejected branch) |
     | Only channel configured, ok | Anonymized post sent | yes |
     | Only channel configured, fails | Throws | yes |
     | Only private configured | Private message only | yes |
     | Nothing configured | One existing "disabled" warn | yes |
     | Token set, neither id set | Only the "disabled" warn | yes |
     | Token set, channel only | One "TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID not set" warn per process
  | yes |

     - **Parallelism.** Both `sendTelegramMessage` calls are created inside the
  `Promise.allSettled` array before either is awaited, so the sends are
  parallel.
     - **Deadline.** Each call computes its own 4 s deadline
  (`TOTAL_BUDGET_MS`), and the two run concurrently, so the wall-clock total
  stays at or under 4 s.
     - **Retry.** After a throw the handler retries and resends the channel
  post. This is an acceptable duplicate, and the docs say so.
     - **Warn flag.** It fires only when the chat id is empty and a target
  exists. The disabled path returns before it, so the two warns are never both
  emitted.
  3. **Logs.**
     - The channel log line is `telegram: channel post failed (<message>)`. The
  message is `telegram send failed: <status> <description>` or `<error.name>`,
  and never contains the URL, token, chat id or text.
     - The test asserts the dump does not contain the token, `api.telegram.org`,
  either chat id, the name or the phone. One weakness: `dump()` uses `inspect`
  without `showHidden`, unlike the older tests.
  4. **UI.**
     - **Footer.** The text is "Telegram channel with requests",
  `href={CONTACT.telegramChannelUrl}`, `target="_blank"`, `rel="noopener
  noreferrer"`, the sr-only "(opens in a new tab)" span, and `minHeight:
  44` through the shared `linkSx`.
     - **Success view.** The line "New requests without names and phone numbers are visible in our Telegram channel: " sits after the hours box. The link "Open channel" is
  underlined (`underline="always"`) with the same attributes. It uses
  `colors.ink` (#231A14) on paper or card (#FBF6EC), a contrast ratio above
  14:1.
     - **Focus and live region.** Focus still goes to the success heading
  (`successHeadingRef`, unchanged). The `role="status"` live region is
  untouched.
     - **Failure view.** The "write to us on Telegram" line is removed. The
  phone button and retry button remain.
  5. **Removed fields.**
     - The grep (excluding `node_modules`, `.next`, `.git`, `.claude` and the
  lockfile) finds `telegramHandle`, `telegramUrl`, `kvarta_roastery` or
  `@kvarta` only in `tests/footer.test.tsx`, `tests/lead-form.test.tsx`,
  `tests/telegram.test.ts` and `lib/contact.ts`.
     - In the two tests they appear as absence assertions or unrelated fixtures
  (`@kvarta_orders`). In `lib/contact.ts` it is the Instagram handle
  `@kvarta.roastery`, which is a different value.
     - The guard test scans `app/`, `components/` and `lib/` for `.ts`, `.tsx`
  and `.md` files, so it excludes itself (it lives in `tests/`) and
  `node_modules`. It is not vacuous, because the directories exist and contain
  many files.
  6. **Privacy page.**
     - The "Your rights" sentence now gives only the phone number.
     - The data-collection sentence reads "The request goes to the manager in a private chat. Only anonymous notifications about new requests, without names and phone numbers, are published in Kvarta's Telegram channel." This matches the required text except for
  line wrapping.
  7. **Env and docs.**
     - `.env.example` has `TELEGRAM_CHANNEL_ID=` and empty values.
     - There is no token-shaped string and no `-100…` id except the placeholder
  `-1001234567890`. The real channel id `-100XXXXXXXXXX` appears nowhere.
     - The invite link appears only in `lib/contact.ts` and the tests, plus
  `https://t.me/+<code>` in the docs.
     - The docs cover the invite link not being a chat id, `getUpdates` ids, the
  Start step for the private chat, and revoking a leaked invite link.
  8. **Tests.**
     - The replaced assertion was
  `expect(document.body.textContent).not.toMatch(/Telegram|bot/i)`. It is now an
  exact check of the line copy plus the href, target, rel and text content of
  the link.
     - The `queryByRole('link', { name: /bot/ })` check remains, so no coverage
  is lost. The new failure-view test checks that no write-us text or Telegram
  link appears.
     - The parallel-deadline test fakes the clock and has both targets hang. It
  expects `clock <= 4000` and the error `telegram send failed: TimeoutError`.
  This is meaningful, though it depends on microtask ordering (`await
  Promise.resolve()`).
  9. **Other files.**
     - Outside the expected set: `tests/lead-form.test.tsx` and the new
  `tests/footer.test.tsx`. Both are reasonable.
     - `tests/telegram.test.ts` has unrelated prettier-style reformatting of
  existing tests, which adds diff noise.
     - There is no layout change, no hardcoded hex, and no new `'use client'`.

  ### Strengths
  - The channel post is built from a fixed template, and the private message
  goes only to the private target, as the exact-text tests show.
  - All failure logs and errors are built from safe parts only.
  - The delivery rules are implemented compactly and tested case by case.
  - Copy and accessibility attributes match the spec, and the removed fields are
  guarded by a test.

  ### Issues

  #### Critical (Must Fix)
  - None.

  #### Important (Should Fix)
  1. **No guard for equal chat ids.** If `TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID ===
  TELEGRAM_CHANNEL_ID`, a `.env` mistake publishes the full lead (name and
  phone) in the public channel. The docs only say "never point it there". The
  fix is small:
     - In `sendTelegram`, treat equal ids as misconfiguration. Send only the
  anonymized post to that id, drop the private target, and warn once without the
  id.
     - Add a test with equal ids that asserts no sent body contains the name or
  phone.

  #### Minor (Nice to Have)
  1. `formatChannelMessage` should fall back safely (a constant label) when
  `interest` is not in the map, instead of printing "undefined".
  2. For an invalid `createdAt`, `formatMinskTime` echoes the raw string into
  the public post. Use a placeholder in the channel formatter.
  3. The privacy page says details go to a closed chat, but that is true only
  once `TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID` is set. Until then leads are only stored. The docs
  should say explicitly that with no `TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID` details are not
  delivered anywhere except storage (the troubleshooting table only covers the
  channel-only case).
  4. The channel-failure log test should use `showHidden` like the older tests.
  A "both fail" test case is also missing.
  5. The unrelated reformatting of existing tests in `tests/telegram.test.ts` is
  noise.

  ### Assessment
  **Task quality:** Needs fixes
  **Reasoning:** The implementation matches the spec, and the privacy and
  delivery traces hold, with all the unit tests passing. But a single `.env`
  mistake (the same id in both variables) would put customer phones in the
  public channel, and no guard stops it, so it must be fixed before this can be
  trusted.

› Message from general-purpose
  ### Finding Verdicts
  - **Finding 1: Delivery `maxWidth: 560` applies at all widths (640-1023
  change)**: ADDRESSED. `components/Delivery.tsx:58` now reads `maxWidth: { md:
  560 }`. Breakpoints are `{ xs: 0, sm: 640, md: 1024, lg: 1200, xl: 1536 }`
  (theme/theme.ts:58), so xs and sm get no max width. The grid is single-column
  below 1024 (`gridTemplateColumns: { xs: 'minmax(0, 1fr)', md: 'repeat(2, ...)'
  }`), so the cap can only bind in the two-column layout. Numbers: at 1024 the
  container is 944, the gap is 24, the column is 460 and the card inner width is
  396 (under 560, no-op). At 1440 the container is 1200, the column is 588 and
  the inner width is 524 (no-op). The cap first binds near 1536 (inner ~568),
  which is above 1024 and therefore allowed. At 768, the original cap bit (inner
  624 > 560); the fix removes that.

  - **Minor (a): hero headline widow**: ADDRESSED. `components/Hero.tsx:47` adds
  `'@media (min-width:1441px)': { textWrap: 'balance' }` to the h1 sx only.
  Nothing is added at 1440 or below. The copy and the nbsp are unchanged (the
  diff touches only that sx line). The `@media` key in sx matches the existing
  pattern in `components/Week.tsx:83`. Browsers without `text-wrap` ignore it,
  so no fallback is needed. Minor note: the `1441px` boundary is a strict `>
  1440`, which is what the spec asked for.

  - **Minor (b): textarea `minHeight: 97` px is pixel-fragile**: ADDRESSED.
  `components/LeadForm.tsx:727` now sets `minHeight: 'calc(3 * 1.4375em +
  28px)'`. Checks:
    - The textarea font is 16 px, set by `inputSx.fontSize: 16`
  (LeadForm.tsx:130). The MUI InputBase default `lineHeight: '1.4375em'` is at
  node_modules/@mui/material/InputBase/InputBase.js:82, with `font: 'inherit'`
  at line 146.
    - So 1.4375em = 23 px, and 3 × 23 + 28 = 97 px at default size. That matches
  the old value, so there is no change at default settings.
    - The `& textarea` padding is `14px 16px` (px, LeadForm.tsx:727), so the 28
  px term is consistent with a padding that does not scale.
    - At a forced 20 px font: 3 × 28.75 + 28 = 114.25 px, which matches the
  report's 114.
    - The selector `textarea:not([aria-hidden])` is the visible one only. The
  MUI autosize shadow textarea is not affected, and `boxSizing: border-box` is
  kept.
    - Sizing only: no logic change.
    - Caveat: `inputSx` fixes the font at 16 px, so this em term does not
  currently change with the user's root font setting. It is a robustness change,
  not a fix for a live bug.

  - **Minor (c): bag at 2560 should fill its column sensibly, and 1440
  unchanged**: ADDRESSED. `components/BagIllustration.tsx:31` changes to
  `wide(469, 0.5, 900)`. The helper is `clamp(base, base + (100vw-1440)*perPx,
  cap)` (components/Section.tsx:18-19, read from the worktree). Column =
  `(container − gap) × 469 / 1109`, where the hero grid is `640fr 469fr`
  (Hero.tsx:27), the container is `CONTAINER_PX` (Section.tsx:12-16), and the
  gap is `wide(48, 0.05, 96)` (Hero.tsx:29). The bag wrapper is end-aligned at
  md (Hero.tsx:121).

    | Width | Container | Gap | Column | Old max (0.35, 760) | New max (0.5,
  900) | Rendered bag |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | 1024 | 944 | 48 | 379 | 469 | 469 | 379 (unchanged) |
    | 1440 | 1200 | 48 | 487 | 469 | 469 | 469 (identical) |
    | 1680 | 1425.6 | 60 | 577.5 | 553 | 589 | 577.5 (fills column) |
    | 1920 | 1651.2 | 72 | 668 | 637 | 709 | 668 (fills column) |
    | 2560 | 2252.8 | 96 | 912 | 760 | 900 | 900 (12 px slack, end-aligned) |
    | 3440 | 3080 | 96 | 1262 | 760 | 900 | 900 (362 px empty, end-aligned) |

    At 2560 the bag sits inside the column. At 3440 it stays at the right edge
  with empty space to its left, which is acceptable but not measured visually.
  The clamp's lower bound is 469 for all widths ≤1440, so nothing below 1440
  changes.

› Message from general-purpose
  ### Finding Verdicts
  - **Equal chat ids publish the full lead to the public channel (Important)** —
  ADDRESSED. The guard is in `lib/telegram.ts` (`sameChatId` and the
  `privateChat = ""` branch in `sendTelegram`). I ran the full suite on an
  extracted copy of cc2a27d under the scratchpad (the named worktree no longer
  exists): 57/57 pass. The full-message path is unreachable when ids are equal,
  because `privateChat` is blanked before the `Promise.allSettled`.

    Hand-trace of the comparison (private, channel), using the same logic run in
  node:
    - ('-1001234567890', '-1001234567890'): equal. One fetch, channel only,
  anonymized body.
    - (' -1001234567890 ', '-1001234567890'): equal, because both are trimmed.
    - ('-1001234567890', '-01001234567890'): equal. Both match `^-?\d+$`, so
  BigInt compares them. Leading zeros do not bypass the guard.
    - ('-1001234567890', '-1001234567890.0'): not equal. Telegram would reject
  `.0` as a chat_id, so this is not a leak route.
    - ('@KvartaOrders', '@kvartaorders'): equal, case-insensitive.
    - ('@kvartaorders', 'kvartaorders'): not equal. A missing '@' is not
  normalized. This is fine: Telegram rejects a bare name as a chat_id (my
  understanding of the Bot API, not tested).
    - ('', ''): the early return fires on `!privateChat && !channel`. No guard,
  no crash, one "disabled" warn.
    - ('123', ''): private only. The channel is skipped and the
  "TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID not set" branch is not entered.
    - ('', '-100…'): channel only, with the one "not set" warn.
    - ('+100…', '-100…') and ('+100…', '100…'): not equal. This is a theoretical
  bypass only if Telegram parsed a leading '+' as an integer, which I believe
  it does not. See the Minor below.
    - ('-0', '0'): equal.
    - Whitespace-only env values are now trimmed to empty, which counts as not
  configured.

  - **Warn exactly once, fixed text, no id** — ADDRESSED. The `warnedSameId`
  module flag is set on first use. The text is the fixed string in
  `lib/telegram.ts:150-158`. Tests reset state with `vi.resetModules()` in
  `beforeEach` plus a dynamic `import("@/lib/telegram")`. No test-only export
  was added, which I think is the right call. The test calls `sendTelegram`
  twice per module instance and asserts exactly one matching warn. It also
  asserts that `dump()` does not contain `CHANNEL` or `Kvarta_Feed`.

  - **Equal-id delivery outcomes** — ADDRESSED. Each of the three id-pair cases
  makes 2 `sendTelegram` calls and expects 2 fetches in total, so one fetch per
  call. `bodies()[0].text` is asserted equal to `formatChannelMessage(lead())`.
  The test runs `inspect(fetchMock.mock.calls, { showHidden: true, depth: 10 })`
  over all recorded requests and asserts it contains neither "Anna",
  "375291234567", "29 123" nor "I want beans". The "throws when that single post
  fails" test asserts the exact message `telegram send failed: 400 Bad Request`.

  - **Minor 1: unknown interest** — ADDRESSED. `INTEREST_LABELS[...] ??
  "request"`. The test asserts the exact string and the absence of "undefined".

  - **Minor 2: invalid createdAt** — ADDRESSED. `Number.isNaN(new
  Date(createdAt).getTime())` gives "time unknown". The test passes the raw
  string `"<i>+375291234567</i>"` and asserts the exact output, with no echo.
  `formatMinskTime` is untouched (not in the diff), so the private message and
  the valid-input tests are unchanged. The empty-string case also yields NaN. A
  non-string such as a number would not be flagged as invalid (`new
  Date(number)` is valid), and one such as `undefined` would give NaN. This is
  not tested but is harmless.

  - **Minor 3: docs** — ADDRESSED. `docs/telegram-channel.md` now says that
  without `TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID`, order details are not delivered anywhere except
  the stored data. It also says the privacy-page closed-chat claim only holds
  once `TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID` is set. There are two troubleshooting rows (missing
  chat id, equal ids), and the safety-net sentence sits next to the "never
  point" line. The real channel id `100XXXXXXXXXX` appears 0 times in the diff.

  - **Minor 4: 'both targets fail' test** — ADDRESSED. It asserts the exact
  message `"telegram send failed: 400 Bad Request"` and 2 fetches.

  - **Minor 5: channel-failure log test with `showHidden: true`** — ADDRESSED.
  `dump()` now uses `showHidden: true`.

  - **Minor 6: revert test churn** — ADDRESSED. `git diff 7a2f9fa cc2a27d --
  tests/telegram.test.ts` (the pre-task version against the fix head) shows 275
  insertions and 0 deletions. The deletions visible in the 96e420d..cc2a27d diff
  only undo the earlier prettier churn, which restores the original formatting.

  ### New Breakage in the Fix Diff
  - **Minor** — `tests/telegram.test.ts` ("treats a numeric id…"):
  `"012345".slice(1)` is just "12345", so this case is identical strings and
  does not exercise the leading-zero or BigInt path. The BigInt path works
  (traced above), but it has no test.
  - **Minor** — `lib/telegram.ts` `sameChatId`: a '+'-prefixed id such as
