› Message from general-purpose
  task-17 status: DONE. SQLite file store (better-sqlite3) is the only store;
  Upstash fully removed; deployment package and docs written; all checks pass.
  Branch worktree-agent-a741a5819aed8bca2, 6 commits on top of 292726e (the last
  is the Telegram test you asked for).

  ## Commits
  - 5260520 Add the SQLite file store behind the lead store contract.
  - dcaec09 Use the SQLite file as the only store and remove Upstash.
  - bafb95d Add lead export, backup and purge scripts.
  - a427570 Add the standalone build, Docker and nginx deployment files.
  - ea95564 Document the server deployment.
  - 67ca96d Test that creating an order stores the lead and posts to Telegram.

  ## What was built
  - **Store:** `lib/file-store.ts` is `createFileStore({path, now})`. It runs
  WAL, busy_timeout 5000, synchronous FULL, and `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` for every
  write. The schema is versioned with `user_version`. Expired rows are purged in
  the same transactions (50 per table per call); leads are never purged. The
  directory is 0700, the DB file and its -wal/-shm are 0600. Open errors are
  clear and wrapped.
  - **Selection:** `lib/get-store.ts` returns a lazy singleton file store.
  `LEADS_DB_PATH` is optional and defaults to `<cwd>/data/leads.sqlite`. If
  `VERCEL` is set and `ALLOW_EPHEMERAL_FILE_STORE` is not `1`, it logs one error
  and every method rejects, so the API answers 503. An unopenable file does the
  same. The route calls `getStore()` lazily, so importing it never touches the
  disk.
  - **Upstash removal:**
    - Code and tests: removed `createUpstashStore`, the Lua scripts, all Upstash
  mocks and tests, and the `@upstash/redis` dependency. `.env.example` no
  longer lists the Upstash variables.
    - Greps: `package.json` and the lockfile have 0 matches, and the string
  `upstash` does not appear in `.next` or in the server logs. A repo-wide grep
  still matches only the old superseded design spec and plan under
  docs/superpowers, plus the addendum I added to the design-delta spec.
  - **Memory store:** it now implements the 7-day TTL on the notification
  marker, so it matches the contract.
  - **Scripts** (npm `leads:export`, `leads:backup`, `leads:purge`):
    - `export-leads.mjs`: read-only, CSV or `--json`, with quoting and formula
  guard.
    - `backup-db.mjs`: `VACUUM INTO`, mode 0600, rotation (default 14).
    - `purge-leads.mjs`: `--older-than-days N`, dry run by default, `--apply` to
  delete.
  - **Deployment:** `next.config.ts` has `output: 'standalone'` and
  `serverExternalPackages: ['better-sqlite3']`, with every existing option kept.
  New files: `Dockerfile` (node:22-bookworm-slim, 3 stages, non-root),
  `docker-compose.yml` (read_only root, tmpfs, 127.0.0.1:3000 only),
  `deploy/nginx.conf`, `deploy/kvarta.service`, `deploy/backup.cron`,
  `.dockerignore`, and `data/` plus `*.sqlite*` in `.gitignore`. nginx
  overwrites `X-Forwarded-For` with `$remote_addr`, and the config explains why
  there is no CSP. I did not add `/api/health`; the healthcheck hits `/`.
  - **Docs:** `docs/deploy-server.md` covers the engine justification, why not
  Vercel, the env var table, paths A and B, firewall, TLS, update, backup and
  restore, export, privacy and retention (purge), secret rotation, disk full,
  logs.

  ## Engine choice
  I chose better-sqlite3 13 (`engines` node>=22). It ships prebuilt binaries
  inside the npm tarball, so it needs no compiler and no download. node:sqlite
  is still experimental in Node 22 and prints an ExperimentalWarning every
  start.

  **Gotcha you should know:** npm 10 (bundled with Node 22) runs `node-gyp
  rebuild` for better-sqlite3 when installing from the lockfile, which failed
  inside the Docker image. The Dockerfile and docs therefore use `npm ci
  --ignore-scripts`. Plain `npm ci` worked locally on this machine, but a bare
  server on npm 10 without Python and a compiler would hit the failure.

  ## Verification
  - `npm test`: 22 files, 317 tests pass (before the last test file was added).
  `lead-telegram.test.ts` passes (4/4), and the new store and script tests are
  part of that run. `npm run lint` is clean. `npm ci` ran from scratch.
  - Cold `npm run build` succeeds. `/opengraph-image` and `/twitter-image` are
  static, so the .woff fonts are not needed at runtime. The standalone output
  includes `better-sqlite3`, its linux-x64 and linuxmusl prebuilds, and the
  @fontsource files. There is no `public/` dir in the repo; the docs copy it if
  it exists.
  - **Standalone server** (no storage env at all, started from
  `.next/standalone` with `.next/static` copied in):
    - GET / returns 200.
    - POST /api/lead returns 200 `{ok:true,leadId}`.
    - The same phone with a different key returns 200 with `duplicate:true` and
  the new key only, never another lead's id.
    - A bad phone returns 400.
    - `data/` is 700; `leads.sqlite`, `-wal` and `-shm` are 600.
    - 20 parallel curls with the same phone and distinct keys gave 20 responses
  with 200, 19 of them `duplicate:true`. The export shows exactly one stored
  lead for that phone.
    - After a kill and restart, the same phone within 10 minutes still returns
  `duplicate:true`.
    - The server log has 0 matches for upstash or redis. With the Telegram
  variables unset, only one line is logged ("telegram notifications disabled")
  and no Telegram call is made.
  - **`npm start` with no storage env:** it created `./data/leads.sqlite` under
  the cwd and returned 200, then `duplicate:true` for the same phone. It prints

› Message from general-purpose
  ### Spec Compliance
  - ✅ Spec compliant for the storage layer, selection, Upstash removal and
  scripts. ❌ Three deployment-package defects break steps the docs say will
  work (all listed under Important below):
    - `deploy/backup.cron` never runs.
    - `http2 on;` fails `nginx -t` on stock Debian 12 and Ubuntu nginx.
    - The documented certbot procedure probably leaves an HTTPS redirect loop.
    - `server_tokens off` is missing (Minor).
  - ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff:
    - That the Docker image builds and runs healthy. The implementer reports it
  did; I did not build it.
    - That `docker compose`, nginx, certbot and systemd behave as described.
  None of them was run.
    - Whether Next's runtime cache writes under `ProtectSystem=strict`
  (read-only `/opt`) cause errors on path B.

  ### Strengths
  - **Contract parity holds, traced method by method against `lib/store.ts`:**
    - `claimPhone` returns the existing key, including the caller's own key,
  while `expires_at > t`. Expired rows are purged (`<= t`) and overwritten by
  the upsert inside the same transaction. `ttlSeconds * 1000` and all times are
  in ms throughout.
    - `releasePhone` runs `DELETE ... WHERE phone=? AND lead_id=?`.
    - `saveLead` uses `INSERT OR IGNORE` on the `leads.id` primary key.
    - The notification marker is 604,800,000 ms (7 days). The memory store now
  has the same 7-day TTL.
    - `hitRate` keeps `window_end` unchanged within the window and resets to 1
  after it. Counts match the memory store.
  - **Every mutating method uses better-sqlite3
  `.transaction(...).immediate()`**, which wraps in `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` and rolls
  back on a throw. Sync throws inside `async` become rejections, and
  `handleLead` maps claim/save failures to 503. WAL, busy_timeout and
  synchronous=FULL are set per connection on open. There is no read-then-write
  outside a transaction. `migrate` is idempotent under racing opens.
  - **Multi-process experiment** (`/tmp/claude-1000/-home-usr-projects-coffee/8c
  81cb6b-623b-4837-9e9b-0e0d383d80b2/scratchpad/exp/run.sh`): 12 separate Node
  processes, synchronized start, same file, same phone, distinct keys, 50
  `hitRate` calls each.
    - Exactly one `null`; the other 11 returned that winner's key.
    - `phone_claims` held 1 row and the rate count was exactly 600 (no lost
  updates).
    - With umask 0002: directory 700, `leads.sqlite` / `-wal` / `-shm` all 600.
  - **Files:** the DB file is created with `openSync(file,'a',0o600)` before
  SQLite opens it, so there is no world-readable window. `-wal`/`-shm` inherit
  the main file's mode and are also chmodded. `mkdirSync({recursive,
  mode:0o700})` applies only to directories it creates, so it never chmods an
  existing parent.
  - **Selection:** `getStore()` is a lazy singleton; the route calls it only
  inside `POST`. The Vercel guard logs once (because of the singleton), never
  touches the disk, and returns rejecting stores with PII-free messages.
  - **Upstash is gone** from code, tests, package.json/lockfile (the one
  lockfile "match" is a hash substring) and `.env.example`. It survives only in
  the superseded `docs/superpowers` history plus the new addendum.
  `LEADS_DB_PATH` is documented.
  - **Scripts:**
    - Export and backup open the DB `readonly` + `fileMustExist`.
    - The CSV quotes `"` `,` CR and LF. The formula guard covers `= + - @ \t
  \r`; JSON output is left unmodified.
    - `VACUUM INTO` escapes `'` in the path. Backups are 0600, and rotation
  deletes only files matching `^leads-\d{8}-\d{6}\.sqlite$`.
    - `--keep` must be an integer ≥ 1. Purge is a dry run without `--apply` and
  rejects NaN or days < 1.
    - The "365 days" in the purge docs matches the privacy page's "Up to 12
  months" (up to 12 months).
  - **better-sqlite3 13.0.3** ships `prebuilds/linux-x64.node`, `linuxmusl-*`,
  `linux-arm64` and others, and has no install script. Only `binding.gyp`
  triggers npm's implicit `node-gyp rebuild`, so `npm ci --ignore-scripts` is
  correct, not a broken runtime.
  - **Dockerfile:** non-root `node` user, `/data` 0700 and node-owned,
  healthcheck uses `node -e fetch` (no curl needed). The `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`
  build arg is wired through compose with a `:?` guard. `.dockerignore` excludes
  `.env*`, `data`, `*.sqlite*`, `.git` and `node_modules`. Compose binds
  `127.0.0.1` with read_only, tmpfs and cap_drop ALL.
  - **nginx** overwrites `X-Forwarded-For` with `$remote_addr`.
  - **`tests/lead-telegram.test.ts`** uses a temp DB, stubbed env and fetch with
  a fake token, and restores everything in `afterEach`. All SQL uses bound
  parameters; the only interpolation is the constant table/column names and
  `PURGE_BATCH`.

  ### Issues
  #### Critical (Must Fix)
  None.

  #### Important (Should Fix)
  1. **`deploy/backup.cron:3` – backups never run on path B.**
     - The job runs as user `kvarta` and redirects to `>>
  /var/log/kvarta-backup.log`. `kvarta` cannot create files in root-owned
  `/var/log`, so the shell fails the redirection and never starts node. With no
  local MTA this fails silently.
     - The docs (`docs/deploy-server.md` "Backups and restore") point operators
  to this file as the backup schedule.
     - Fix: log under a kvarta-owned path, e.g.
  `/var/backups/kvarta/backup.log`, or pre-create the log with `install -o
  kvarta -m 0600 /dev/null /var/log/kvarta-backup.log` in the docs.
  2. **`deploy/nginx.conf:241` – `http2 on;` breaks `nginx -t` on the documented
  platforms.**
     - The directive exists only in nginx ≥ 1.25.1. The docs say "Debian or
  Ubuntu" with `apt install nginx`, which gives 1.22 on Debian 12, 1.18 on
  Ubuntu 22.04 and 1.24 on Ubuntu 24.04.
     - Fix: use `listen 443 ssl http2;` (accepted everywhere; newer versions
  only print a deprecation warning), or note the minimum version.
  3. **`docs/deploy-server.md` TLS steps – likely redirect loop.**
     - The steps are: comment out the 443 block, then run `certbot --nginx -d
  ...`. With only the port-80 block present, certbot's installer adds `listen
  443 ssl` and the cert lines to that block, which still contains `return 301
  https://$host$request_uri`.
     - HTTPS requests to that block redirect to themselves. After the 443 block
  is uncommented, the earlier-defined block still wins for the same
  `server_name`.
     - I could not run certbot, so this is reasoned, not observed.
     - Fix: `sudo certbot certonly --nginx -d your.domain` (obtain the
  certificate only), then uncomment the 443 block and reload. Alternatively, use
  `--webroot`.

  #### Minor (Nice to Have)
  - **The "race" test is not concurrent.** `tests/file-store.test.ts` "two
  connections racing" calls the synchronous API through `Promise.all`, so the
  calls run one after another. It proves cross-connection visibility, not
  contention. My multi-process experiment covers the real case; consider a
  child-process test.
  - **The exact expiry boundary is untested.** No test sits at `expires_at ==
  now`, so a `>` to `>=` mutation would survive. The tests use ±1 ms around it.
  - **A failed open is cached until restart.** `getStore` keeps the failing
  store from a transient open failure (e.g. the volume or permissions not ready
  at first request) until the process restarts. The docs say to restart;
  consider retrying the open after a cooldown.
  - **`SQLITE_BUSY` can freeze the server.** With better-sqlite3 being
  synchronous, a busy wait blocks the Node event loop for up to 5 s under
  multi-process contention. Acceptable, but worth a note.
  - **Purged leads stay on disk.** `purge-leads.mjs` deletes rows but not freed
  pages or WAL content. For a privacy promise, consider `PRAGMA
  secure_delete=ON` or a `VACUUM` after `--apply`.
  - **Export output can be world-readable.** `> leads.csv` in the docs creates a
  PII file with the shell's umask (often 0644). Suggest `umask 077` or `install
  -m 600`.
  - **Path B npm cache lands in the data directory.** `npm ci` runs as `kvarta`,
  whose home is `/var/lib/kvarta`, so the npm cache may be written there next
  to the DB. Consider a separate home, or `--cache /tmp/...`.
  - **`EnvironmentFile=` overrides `Environment=` in systemd.** If an operator
  copies `.env.example` (which has `LEADS_DB_PATH=`, empty) into
  `/etc/kvarta/env`, the path falls back to `<standalone>/data` on read-only
  `/opt` and every request gets 503. Say "only the Telegram variables"
  explicitly, or drop the empty line from `.env.example`.
  - **Smaller gaps:**
    - The unit hardcodes `/usr/bin/node`; nvm installs differ.
    - nginx has no `server_tokens off`.
    - The `/_next/static/` location drops X-Frame-Options, Referrer-Policy and
  Permissions-Policy.
    - Compose has no Docker log rotation.
    - `.env.example` lacks `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` (compose fails with a clear
  message).
    - An existing data directory with looser permissions is not tightened;
  Docker and the systemd docs create it 0700, so this is only an edge case.
    - `.dockerignore` does not exclude `.superpowers` (build stage only; not
  copied into the runner).
  - **Tests removed:** the Upstash multi/eval call-shape tests and the
  Upstash-through-handleLead test. The latter was rewritten against the file
  store (failed save releases the claim, the retry stores and notifies once).
  The contract suite now runs the same cases on both stores via `describe.each`.
  Nothing behavioral was lost.

  ### Assessment
  **Task quality:** Needs fixes

  **Reasoning:** The storage layer is correct and matches the memory store's
  contract; one-winner claims and no lost counts held across 12 processes, and
  the files are 0600/0700. The deployment package has three copy-paste breakages
  that the docs present as working: the backup cron never runs, `http2 on`
  fails on stock Debian 12/Ubuntu nginx, and the certbot sequence likely
  produces a redirect loop. All three are small fixes.

  - **backup.cron log under /var/log is not writable by kvarta** — ADDRESSED.

  `docs/deploy-server.md` creates that directory with `install -d -o kvarta -g
  kvarta -m 0700 /var/lib/kvarta /var/backups/kvarta` before the cron copy and
  before the first run. The doc steps run `cp deploy/backup.cron
  /etc/cron.d/kvarta-backup`, then a one-line `sudo -u kvarta sh -c '... >>
  /var/backups/kvarta/backup.log 2>&1' && ls -l /var/backups/kvarta`. User,
  directory and `--dir` agree between the cron file and the docs.
  - **nginx `http2 on;` breaks `nginx -t` on older nginx** — ADDRESSED.
  `deploy/nginx.conf` has `listen 443 ssl http2;` and `listen [::]:443 ssl
  http2;` with a comment about nginx 1.25.1 and newer. No `http2 on;` remains.
  - **certbot --nginx would add a listen 443 to the redirect block and loop** —
  ADDRESSED. The port-80 block serves `/.well-known/acme-challenge/` (root
  `/var/www/certbot`) before `location / { return 301 ... }`. The docs order is
  correct:
    1. `mkdir /var/www/certbot`
    2. Comment out the 443 block, then `nginx -t && reload`.
    3. `certbot certonly --webroot -w /var/www/certbot -d ...`
    4. Uncomment the 443 block, then `nginx -t && reload`.
    5. `certbot renew --dry-run`
    6. A deploy hook in `renewal-hooks/deploy/reload-nginx`, with `chmod +x`.
  - **Minors** — all present:
    - `server_tokens off` is set, and Referrer-Policy, Permissions-Policy and

  notes, and the SQLITE_BUSY sentence.

  in the diff and is unchanged. The ACME location is a plain `root`, so it is
  not an open proxy. The static location keeps its `proxy_pass`.

  Check C (get-store cooldown) is correct.
  - The failing store is served until `retryAt = now + 30 s`.
  - A retry happens only at or after that time, and each failed attempt logs
  once.
  - Success sets `retryAt` to null and is cached.
  - The Vercel guard returns `retryAt: null`, so it logs once, rejects, and
  never retries.
  - The logged value is `safeErrorLabel`, so it is PII-free.
  - The tests use an injected clock and open function and cover the cooldown,
  one log per window, and caching after recovery.

  Check D (purge) is correct.
  - The order is `secure_delete=ON`, then DELETE in autocommit, then `VACUUM`
  outside any transaction, then `wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)`.
  - VACUUM and the checkpoint run only when `deleted > 0`.
  - `--apply` is still required, and the dry-run path returns before the writes,
  so it is unchanged.
  - The test is non-vacuous. It asserts the name IS present in the raw db and
  -wal bytes before the purge, and absent after it. It also reopens the db and
  writes to it.

  `../../lib/file-store.ts` under plain `node`. The only non-erasable import in
  the chain is `import type ... '@/lib/store'`, which type stripping drops, so
  the alias is not a problem. That import needs default type stripping, which
  Node 22 has only from 22.18; 22.6 to 22.17 need a flag. `package.json` has no

  Before deploying, an important warning about storage. Netlify works as a set
  of temporary functions without a persistent disk, so the file database won't
  persist there. Here is how I handle it:
  - Mode. The app works with a temporary database in /tmp and with explicit
    consent to that in the site settings. So duplicate protection and rate
    limiting work only within one instance.
  - Durable record of a request. Only the Telegram message remains. Right now it
    goes to the channel and is anonymous there. Full requests with phone numbers
    will reach the manager in a private chat only after you press Start in
    @KvartaCoffee_bot.

  Remaining caveats:
  - Storage on Netlify is per-instance, non-durable SQLite in `/tmp`. Dedupe and
  rate limiting are best-effort, and data is lost on cold starts. Telegram is
  the only durable record of an order.

  nowhere, not even to the channel.

✻ Churned for 52s · done 23:30

❯ bad performance, move everything you can into server components

● Understood: performance comes down to even the server sections pulling the MUI
  runtime to the client. The reason is that every @mui/material component is
  marked as a client one, so everything built on it is loaded and hydrated in the
  browser. By measurement that is about 196 KB gzip, of which our own code is
  only 7. So real "server-ness" is achieved only by dropping MUI in the page
  markup.

  The plan is this:
  - Server components on CSS Modules. All sections are rewritten on plain HTML
    elements and CSS Modules. Colors come from the same design tokens, the look
    stays the same.
  - Only the live parts stay client-side. That is the form, the text that depends
    on the current time, and the collection of marks.
  - MUI and Emotion are removed from the page entirely.
  - Protection against breakage. Screenshots before and after at 8 widths with a
    byte-by-byte comparison, tests, a Lighthouse measurement before and after.

  The first stage is the foundation and the "before" measurements.

● The quick optimizations are started by one agent with a hard time limit (about
  30–40 minutes). It works like this:
  - "Before" measurement. First it measures the current state: 5 Lighthouse runs,
    the median.
  - Order. It goes down the list: duplicate Emotion styles, deferred form
    hydration, content-visibility for the lower sections, link prefetching and
    small things.

    a real gain stays. The rest is rolled back.

    Tests, a cold-cache build, accessibility and SEO must stay at 100, and the
    scroll to "Contacts" must work.
